Perfect. I've read all 8 investigation files completely. Now let me create a comprehensive analysis of the 6 red flags with the specific evidence, recommendations, and success criteria for each.

## FLUME Red Flag Analysis - Complete Deep Dive

Based on thorough reading of all investigation documents, here are the 6 red flags with precise detail:

---

### **RED FLAG 1: KL Collapse Risk (Training Dynamics)**

**SPECIFIC CONCERN:**
The VAE's posterior distribution might collapse to the prior during training, making the latent variables useless and turning FLUME into a deterministic autoencoder instead of a probabilistic model. This destroys the ability to generate novel reasoning trajectories through sampling.

**EVIDENCE TODAY:**
- FLUME's training logs haven't been analyzed for KL collapse indicators
- No measurements of posterior variance per latent dimension
- No diagnostic plots of KL loss trajectory
- Unknown if β (KL weight) annealing schedule is effective

**INVESTIGATION RECOMMENDS:**
1. **Track KL loss over training** - Plot loss curves to see if KL stays near zero (collapse symptom)
2. **Measure posterior statistics**:
   - Per-dimension variance (should stay >0.5, collapse shows <0.1)
   - Posterior covariance matrix (should be non-trivial structure)
3. **Check reconstruction quality independent of KL** - If reconstruction improves without using latent space, VAE is deterministic
4. **Test sampling diversity** - Decode identical random points multiple times, measure variance
5. **Compare to training with different β schedules**:
   - KL annealing (start β=0, increase slowly)
   - Free bits strategy (allow KL < threshold)
   - Alternative priors (mixture of Gaussians vs. isotropic)

**SUCCESS CRITERIA (Green Light):**
- KL divergence > 0.1 throughout training (not collapsing to zero)
- Posterior variance > 0.5 per dimension (latent space is being used)
- Reconstruction loss doesn't improve significantly if you remove latent variables
- Same decoder input produces different outputs when sampled (stochastic, not deterministic)
- Posterior distribution is notably different from standard Gaussian prior

**FAILURE INDICATORS:**
- KL→0 early in training and stays there
- Posterior variance <0.1 (deterministic)
- Reconstruction quality unchanged if you feed encoder input directly to decoder
- VAE behaves identically to standard autoencoder

**TIME TO RESOLVE:** 20-30 hours (diagnostic experiments)

---

### **RED FLAG 2: Reconstruction Fidelity Unknown (Architecture)**

**SPECIFIC CONCERN:**
When reasoning sequences are compressed to 256D and decompressed, critical semantic information might be lost. We don't know what percentage of reasoning meaning survives the bottleneck. If >30% of semantics are lost, FLUME's whole value proposition fails.

**EVIDENCE TODAY:**
- No measurement of what % of semantics survive VAE compression
- No comparison of original vs. reconstructed trajectories
- No quantification of information loss
- Unknown if 256D is sufficient dimensionality

**INVESTIGATION RECOMMENDS:**
1. **Encode-decode cycles** on test trajectories
2. **Measure multiple loss metrics**:
   - Token-level BLEU (do we recover same tokens?)
   - Semantic similarity using embeddings (do sequences mean the same thing?)
   - Trajectory coherence (do decoded trajectories follow same reasoning logic?)
3. **Analyze failure by complexity**:
   - Group trajectories by length and complexity
   - Measure reconstruction quality per group
   - Identify which reasoning types are hardest to reconstruct
4. **Identify lossy dimensions**:
   - Which latent dimensions hurt semantics if removed?
   - Can we isolate semantic-loss causing dimensions?
5. **Test edge cases**:
   - Very short trajectories (should be lossless)
   - Very long trajectories (where loss likely)
   - Novel reasoning patterns not in training data

**SUCCESS CRITERIA (Green Light):**
- >70% semantic preservation (measured by entailment/similarity metrics)
- BLEU score >0.65 on reconstructed trajectories
- Decoded trajectories pass semantic coherence checks >80% of time
- No systematic loss of specific reasoning types
- Reconstruction quality stable across domain variations

**FAILURE INDICATORS:**
- <50% semantic preservation
- BLEU <0.45
- Novel reasoning patterns completely garbled after decode
- Loss patterns show systematic biases (certain reasoning types always fail)
- Longer trajectories show catastrophic degradation

**TIME TO RESOLVE:** 40-60 hours (building evaluation metrics + analysis)

---

### **RED FLAG 3: Synthetic Quality Unknown (Training)**

**SPECIFIC CONCERN:**
FLUME's ability to generate novel, coherent reasoning trajectories is unvalidated. Generated samples might be "mode collapse" (all similar) or "gibberish" (semantically incoherent). Without this working, FLUME can't provide useful reasoning guidance to agents.

**EVIDENCE TODAY:**
- No evaluation of FLUME-generated trajectories
- No comparison to training data quality
- No human evaluation of synthetic samples
- Unknown diversity of generated reasoning

**INVESTIGATION RECOMMENDS:**
1. **Generate large sample** of synthetic trajectories from FLUME (1000-10000 samples)
2. **Evaluate trajectory quality**:
   - Semantic coherence: Do steps follow logically? (Can use entailment model)
   - Strategic progress: Does reasoning move toward goal?
   - Realistic patterns: Do they match training data patterns?
   - Novelty: Are samples new or memorized from training?
3. **Measure mode coverage**:
   - Do synthetic trajectories cover diverse reasoning approaches?
   - Compare reasoning type distribution in synthetic vs. training data
   - Measure Wasserstein distance between distributions
4. **Test causality**:
   - Does reasoning respect logical causality?
   - Do later steps reference undefined earlier concepts?
   - Do trajectories contradict themselves?
5. **Get human evaluation**:
   - Show humans synthetic trajectories mixed with real ones
   - Can they distinguish? (If 50% accuracy = indistinguishable = success)
   - What problems do they see?

**SUCCESS CRITERIA (Green Light):**
- >60% of synthetic trajectories rated as coherent (by automated metric)
- >80% classified as semantically coherent by human evaluators
- <30% are duplicates/memorized from training (>70% novelty)
- Reasoning type distribution matches training data (Wasserstein distance <0.2)
- Causal logic preserved >90% of time

**FAILURE INDICATORS:**
- <30% coherent (mostly gibberish)
- <50% novelty (just memorizing training data)
- Strong mode collapse (all samples look similar)
- Systematic causal violations (steps don't follow logically)
- Humans easily distinguish synthetic from real (synthetic are obviously bad)

**TIME TO RESOLVE:** 40-60 hours (generation + evaluation)

---

### **RED FLAG 4: Evaluation Metrics Unvalidated (Evaluation)**

**SPECIFIC CONCERN:**
JourneyTracker and DegradationDetector might not measure what we think. They could be systematically biased, measuring something unrelated to actual reasoning quality. If evaluation metrics are invalid, we can't trust any of FLUME's assessments.

**EVIDENCE TODAY:**
- No validation that JourneyTracker scores correlate with actual task performance
- No audit of what DegradationDetector actually computes
- No measurement of metric inter-agreement (do different evals agree?)
- No comparison to human judgment of reasoning quality
- No false positive/negative analysis

**INVESTIGATION RECOMMENDS:**
1. **Audit metric computation**:
   - Read the source code carefully
   - Understand exactly what each metric computes
   - Look for potential biases or false signals
2. **Test on synthetic cases** with known properties:
   - Perfect trajectory → what score?
   - Incoherent trajectory → what score?
   - Real human reasoning → what score?
3. **Correlation analysis**:
   - Collect agents with known performance levels
   - Measure JourneyTracker/DegradationDetector scores for each
   - Compute correlation to actual task success
   - What's R²? (Higher = more predictive)
4. **Measure agreement**:
   - Do different evaluation metrics agree with each other?
   - What's Pearson correlation between JourneyTracker and DegradationDetector?
   - Where do they disagree systematically?
5. **Test domain transfer**:
   - Apply metrics to domain they weren't trained on
   - Do they still work?
   - Are there systematic biases per domain?
6. **Find false positives/negatives**:
   - Trajectories that metric praises but are actually bad
   - Trajectories that metric penalizes but are actually good
   - What patterns cause metric failure?

**SUCCESS CRITERIA (Green Light):**
- Correlation with task performance >0.7 (R² >0.5)
- Inter-metric agreement >0.7 (Pearson correlation)
- Domain transfer: Metrics work similarly on new domains
- False positive rate <10% (metrics don't praise bad reasoning)
- False negative rate <10% (metrics don't penalize good reasoning)
- Domain-specific validation: Metrics transfer with <20% accuracy loss

**FAILURE INDICATORS:**
- <0.5 correlation with task performance (don't predict what matters)
- Metrics wildly disagree with each other
- Heavy domain bias (work on training domain, fail on test domain)
- Systematic false positives (praise obviously bad reasoning)
- Humans disagree with metric scores (metric measuring something else)

**TIME TO RESOLVE:** 30-40 hours (auditing + validation experiments)

---

### **RED FLAG 5: Integration Impact Unknown (Integration)**

**SPECIFIC CONCERN:**
FLUME guidance might not actually help agents, or could even hurt performance by adding overhead without benefit. We don't know if the agent-FLUME coupling is tight and beneficial, or if FLUME is just a computational sink.

**EVIDENCE TODAY:**
- No comparison of agent performance with vs. without FLUME
- No measurement of information flow from FLUME to action selection
- No quantification of how much agent behavior depends on FLUME
- Unknown latency cost of FLUME encoding/decoding
- No characterization of what actions FLUME can induce

**INVESTIGATION RECOMMENDS:**
1. **Ablation study**:
   - Train agent WITH FLUME guidance
   - Train agent WITHOUT FLUME (uses environment state directly)
   - Train random baseline
   - Compare performance curves (steps to reach 90% optimal)
   - Measure sample efficiency: How many environment steps needed?
2. **Information flow analysis**:
   - Trace exactly how FLUME embeddings become actions
   - Measure mutual information between FLUME and actions
   - What % of agent behavior comes from FLUME vs. environment vs. policy?
   - Is coupling deterministic or stochastic?
3. **Coupling strength test**:
   - Vary FLUME quality (use FLUME with different training)
   - Measure sensitivity: ΔPerformance / ΔFLUMEQuality
   - How tight is the coupling?
4. **Latency/memory profile**:
   - Profile encoding latency (trajectory → 256D embedding)
   - Profile decoding latency (embedding → action)
   - Total end-to-end cost vs. environment step time
   - Memory footprint
5. **Action coverage**:
   - Sample FLUME embeddings across latent space
   - What % of valid actions can FLUME induce?
   - Are there action subsets FLUME never generates?
   - Can agent explore properly with FLUME guidance?
6. **Robustness**:
   - Add noise to FLUME embeddings, measure performance degradation
   - What happens if FLUME is unavailable?
   - Graceful degradation or catastrophic failure?

**SUCCESS CRITERIA (Green Light):**
- Agent with FLUME reaches 90% performance 10-50% faster than without
- FLUME contributes >20% of agent behavior (high coupling)
- Latency <100ms per decision (real-time feasible)
- Action coverage >80% of valid action space
- Graceful degradation: Small FLUME errors → small performance drop

**FAILURE INDICATORS:**
- FLUME helps agent <5% (negligible benefit)
- Agent performs WORSE with FLUME (overhead without benefit)
- Latency >500ms (too slow for real-time use)
- Action coverage <50% (FLUME locks agent into few behaviors)
- Catastrophic failure if FLUME is bad (no robustness)

**TIME TO RESOLVE:** 50-80 hours (training multiple agents + analysis)

---

### **RED FLAG 6: Universes Team Fit Unclear (Alignment)**

**SPECIFIC CONCERN:**
FLUME might be cool technology but solve no actual problem the Universes team cares about. Could be completely misaligned with their research needs. This is a career risk—building the wrong thing for the wrong audience.

**EVIDENCE TODAY:**
- Unclear what Universes team's core research problems are
- No understanding of their evaluation needs
- No knowledge of their training environment requirements
- Unknown if FLUME addresses their actual pain points
- Could be solving yesterday's problem or non-problem

**INVESTIGATION RECOMMENDS:**
1. **Research their papers and work**:
   - What problems do they work on?
   - What evaluation frameworks do they use?
   - What tools do they build?
   - What's the trajectory of their research?
2. **Map potential intersection**:
   - For each Universes team research area, ask: Does FLUME help?
   - Rate alignment on scale of (None, Supplementary, Important, Critical)
   - Identify top 3-5 high-impact opportunities
3. **Assess timing**:
   - Is reasoning-based RL a focus area for them NOW?
   - Is it future planning or current priority?
   - Are competitors already solving this?
4. **Understand their constraints**:
   - What's their evaluation budget?
   - What computational resources do they have?
   - What infrastructure integration challenges exist?
   - What would make FLUME easy to use for them?
5. **Get direct feedback**:
   - Present FLUME to team (Phase 1 validation results)
   - Ask: "Does this solve a problem you care about?"
   - Listen for genuine interest vs. politeness
6. **Identify failure modes**:
   - Could FLUME be solving their problem WRONG?
   - Could there be better approaches they prefer?
   - Could FLUME interfere with their existing infrastructure?

**SUCCESS CRITERIA (Green Light):**
- Clear alignment between FLUME and at least 2-3 Universes team projects
- Team expresses genuine interest (not just courtesy)
- FLUME solves a problem they identify as important NOW (not future)
- Natural integration path with their infrastructure exists
- Multiple team members see value (not just one person)

**FAILURE INDICATORS:**
- Team is politely uninterested (genuine red flag)
- FLUME solves wrong problem (solves A, but they need B)
- Integration would require them to change their workflow significantly
- Competitive approaches already exist that they prefer
- Team is focused on different research direction entirely

**TIME TO RESOLVE:** Low direct cost (mostly conversations + research), but CRITICAL for career positioning

|----------|----------|-------------------|------------------|--------------|
| 1. KL Collapse | Critical | 20-30 hrs | KL>0.1, Variance>0.5 | KL→0, Deterministic VAE |
| 2. Reconstruction Fidelity | Critical | 40-60 hrs | >70% Semantic Preservation | <50% Preservation |
| 3. Synthetic Quality | Critical | 40-60 hrs | >80% Coherent, >70% Novel | Gibberish or Mode Collapse |
| 4. Metrics Unvalidated | High | 30-40 hrs | R²>0.5 with Performance | Metrics Don't Predict Quality |
| 5. Integration Impact | High | 50-80 hrs | +5% to +50% Agent Performance | No Benefit or Overhead |
| 6. Universes Team Fit | High | Conversation-based | Clear Alignment & Interest | Wrong Problem for Team |

After all six investigations, FLUME is "green" only if:

- ✅ >70% semantic preservation in reconstructions (Red Flag 2)
- ✅ >80% coherent synthetic trajectories (Red Flag 3)
- ✅ Evaluation metrics correlate >0.7 with performance (Red Flag 4)
- ✅ FLUME improves agent performance by 5-50% (Red Flag 5)
- ✅ Clear alignment with Universes team needs (Red Flag 6)

If **multiple critical failures** are discovered, the recommendation is to **pivot or pause**, not push forward. The roadmap emphasizes that honest negative results are more valuable than false confidence.

    logger.handlers.clear()
    logger.filters.clear()

**Why This Matters:**
- VAE checkpoints can cause NaN propagation across tests if not reset
- RL policy maintains state; stale state breaks isolation
- Logger filters corrupt LogRecord.args (convert int to str, breaking %d formatting)

## FLUME VAE Implementation Thorough Analysis

Based on my comprehensive exploration of the Cohezion codebase, I've identified the complete FLUME VAE architecture and its integration points. Here's the detailed breakdown:

---

## 1. FLUME VAE CORE IMPLEMENTATION

### Architecture Files
- **`/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/src/cohezion/flume/autoencoder.py`** (401 lines)
  - **`ThoughtEncoder`**: Text tokens → transformer → 256D latent vector `z`
  - **`ThoughtDecoder`**: 256D latent `z` → transformer decoder → reconstructed tokens
  - **`FlumeEncoder`** (PreTrainedModel): Full autoencoder combining encoder + decoder
  
### VAE Architecture Details (from `autoencoder.py`)
```python
# Encoder pipeline
Embedding (vocab_size -> embed_dim=256)
+ PositionalEmbedding
→ TransformerEncoder (2 layers, 4 heads, hidden_dim=512)
→ MeanPooling over sequence
→ Linear projection to z_dim=256

# Decoder pipeline (teacher-forcing)
Embedding + PositionalEmbedding
→ TransformerDecoder (takes memory from z_proj)
→ Final projection to vocab logits

# Loss function (line 264-286)
- Reconstruction loss: Cross-entropy (next-token prediction)
- NO explicit KL divergence term (this is NOT a true VAE)
- NO reparameterization trick
- NO latent space regularization
```

**CRITICAL RED FLAG #1**: The `FlumeEncoder` class is **NOT actually a VAE** despite the naming. It's a vanilla autoencoder without:
- KL divergence regularization
- Reparameterization trick (`mu` + `log_var`)
- Latent space prior matching
- Variational training objective (ELBO)

---

## 2. TRAINING PIPELINE MISMATCH

### Training Implementation (`/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/src/cohezion/flume/training.py`)

The **actual VAE** is in `FlumeVAETrainer` (not `FlumeEncoder`):

```python
# Architecture (lines 60-87)
self.encoder = nn.Sequential(
    nn.Linear(z, hidden=512),     # 256 → 512
    nn.ReLU(),
    nn.Linear(512, 512)            # 512 → 512
)
self.mu_head = nn.Linear(512, 256)      # Output space
self.logvar_head = nn.Linear(512, 256)  # Output space
self.decoder = nn.Sequential(
    nn.Linear(256, 512),
    nn.ReLU(),
    nn.Linear(512, 512),
    nn.ReLU(),
    nn.Linear(512, 256)            # Back to 256D
)
```

### Loss Function (lines 104-130)
```python
mse = F.mse_loss(recon, x)              # Reconstruction loss
kl = -0.5 * sum(1 + log_var - mu^2 - exp(log_var))  # KL divergence
coherence_loss = mean((mu_mean - 0.5)^2)  # HIHO regularization

total = mse + kl_weight*0.1*KL + coherence_weight*0.05*coherence
```

**CRITICAL RED FLAG #2**: **TWO INCOMPATIBLE VAE IMPLEMENTATIONS**
- `FlumeEncoder` in `autoencoder.py`: Plain text autoencoder, NO VAE mechanics
- `FlumeVAETrainer` in `training.py`: ACTUAL VAE with KL + reparameterization, trained on **256D latent vectors** (not text)

These are **separate models** operating on different input spaces. The training data pipeline is broken.

---

## 3. TRAINING DATA & PIPELINE

### Data Sources (`experience_collector.py` + `dataset.py`)

**Tier 1: Parquet shards** (`data/journeys/shard_*.parquet`)
- Contains: `state_trajectory`, `mission_id`, `agent_id`, `phi_score`, `novelty`
- Normalized to 12D trajectory + smoothness + convergence scores

**Tier 2: SurrealDB** (`mission_journey` table)
- Async query fallback if parquet unavailable

**Tier 3: Vault** (`~/vaults/cohezion-vault/experiments/*.json`)
- Experiment records as JSON

**Fallback**: Synthetic data (line 86-93 in `dataset.py`)
```python
if not real_data:
    vec = rng.normal(0.5, 0.15, (256,))  # HIHO-centered (0.5 mean)
```

### "Reasoning Trajectories" Unknown
- NO clear definition of what "reasoning trajectories" are
- `state_trajectory` in parquet assumed to be 12D journey points
- `ExperienceCollector._compute_trajectory_stats()` extracts last 12D point + smoothness
- **INPUT TO VAE**: 256D experience vectors (from `ExperienceEncoder`, see section 5)

## 4. INTEGRATION WITH AGENTS: MISSING CONNECTION

### Where FLUME Output Is Used

**Semantic Cache (Production Path)**: `/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/src/cohezion/swarm/semantic_cache.py`
```python
class FlumeVAEEmbeddingModel(EmbeddingModel):
    """256D semantic embedding for fuzzy cache matching."""
    
    async def encode(self, text: str) -> EmbeddingResult:
        if vae_available:
            embedding = vae_encoder.encode(text)  # 256D embedding
            return EmbeddingResult(embedding=embedding.tolist())
        else:
            return hash_fallback_embed(text)  # SHA-256 hash
```

**SemanticCache** uses embeddings for **cosine similarity fuzzy matching**:
- Threshold: 0.88 for VAE embeddings, 0.25 for hash
- Cache hit if similarity > threshold
- **PURPOSE**: L2 cache tier between L1 (exact hash) and L3 (persistent)

### **CRITICAL RED FLAG #3: NO ACTION SPACE MAPPING**
The 256D embeddings from FLUME are **ONLY used for cache similarity matching**. They do NOT:
- Guide agent actions
- Influence skill selection
- Modulate execution parameters
- Connect to swarm routing

**The latent space is purely for semantic discrimination, NOT agent control.**

---

## 5. EVALUATION & METRICS (Mostly Absent)

### What's Actually Measured

**JourneyTracker** (`compound/journey_tracker.py`, 648 lines):
- Takes execution result + task description
- Generates **deterministic 2048D embedding** from task text (SHA-256 + sine modulation)
- Projects to **12D axiomatic space** (chunk-mean: 2048→16 chunks→12D via interpolation)
- Applies operation-specific **modulation profiles** (12D vectors per operation type)
- Computes **phi score**: `0.5*coherence + 0.3*smoothness + 0.2*convergence`

**NO actual FLUME VAE output is tracked here.** The 12D trajectory is generated purely from the task description text hash.

### DegradationDetector (`compound/degradation_detector.py`)
Monitors:
- Cache hit rate (alert if <50%)
- Token efficiency (alert if >10% drop)
- Coherence (alert if <0.60)
- Duration (alert if >25% slowdown)

**Reconstruction fidelity is NOT monitored.** No metrics on VAE quality.

### Tests (`tests/test_flume_training.py`)

What IS tested (5 test classes):
1. **Training convergence**: MSE + total loss decrease over epochs ✓
2. **Checkpoint save/load**: State dict completeness ✓
3. **Latent interpolation**: Decoded outputs don't collapse ✓
4. **Synthetic dataset**: Distribution ~N(0.5, 0.15) ✓
5. **Reconstruction quality**: MSE < 0.5 after 10 epochs ✓

**What is NOT tested:**
- Actual text encoding/decoding (only 256D latent vectors)
- FLUME semantic embeddings for cache (no tests!)
- Integration with JourneyTracker
- Integration with degradation detector
- Action generation from latent vectors (because it doesn't exist)

---

## 6. CRITICAL UNKNOWNS (The 6 Red Flags)

### Red Flag #1: Dual VAE Architecture
**`FlumeEncoder` (text autoencoder) vs `FlumeVAETrainer` (latent VAE)**
- Two different models
- Operate on different input spaces
- Training data pipeline doesn't connect them
- Which one is actually used in production?

### Red Flag #2: No Real Data
**Training data is synthetic or missing real "reasoning trajectories"**
- Parquet tier expects `data/journeys/shard_*.parquet` (likely doesn't exist in deployed system)
- SurrealDB tier requires live connection (not available during training)
- Fallback generates synthetic N(0.5, 0.15) samples
- **VAE trained on synthetic data → distributions match only synthetic data**

### Red Flag #3: Latent Space Purpose Unclear
**256D latent space is used for cache similarity, NOT agent control**
- No documented "action space" mapping
- Morphospace/Bioelectric modules exist but don't connect to trained FLUME
- 12D trajectory from JourneyTracker is text-hash-derived, not FLUME-derived
- **Latent space quality is not observable in agent behavior**

### Red Flag #4: No Reconstruction Quality Metrics
**VAE output quality is never validated post-training**
- Training loss converges but no validation on held-out test set
- Reconstruction MSE not monitored during inference
- No latent space interpolation quality checks in production
- **Silent degradation possible**

### Red Flag #5: HIHO Coherence Constraint Dubious
**Coherence regularization in training (line 118-120 in `training.py`)**
```python
mu_mean = mu.mean(dim=-1)
coherence_loss = mean((mu_mean - 0.5)^2)  # Penalize if mu_mean ≠ 0.5
```
- Penalizes batch-level mean, NOT per-sample coherence
- Assumes 0.5 is globally correct (HIHO) but no validation
- **Constraint may prevent learning meaningful latent structure**

### Red Flag #6: No Experience Encoder → VAE Training Connection
**`ExperienceEncoder` (256D from execution) is separate from `FlumeVAETrainer` training**
- ExperienceEncoder produces: [12D trajectory | 12 metrics | 5 op type | 227 fingerprint]
- FlumeVAETrainer expects: random N(0.5, 0.15) samples
- **They don't interface**: no code path that feeds encoded experiences → VAE training

## 9. CONCLUSIONS FOR PLANNING

The FLUME VAE system has **critical architectural misalignment**:

1. **Two VAE implementations** that don't work together
2. **Training data pipeline broken** (experiences → 256D not wired to VAE trainer)
3. **Latent space is purely for cache matching**, not agent control
4. **Evaluation metrics minimal** (no reconstruction quality, no semantic discriminability tests)
5. **Complex components (morphospace, bioelectric, LCSP) appear unused** in production
6. **HIHO coherence constraint** may prevent meaningful learning

The system works as a **cache acceleration mechanism** (fuzzy matching via embeddings), but NOT as a **reasoning latent space** for agent control or skill routing.

## The Real Situation

The investigation reports frame 6 red flags as "unknowns to measure." But the codebase tells a different story — several of these aren't measurement gaps, they're **architectural fractures**:

1. **There are two incompatible VAE implementations.** `FlumeEncoder` in `autoencoder.py` is a text autoencoder (tokens → 256D → tokens). `FlumeVAETrainer` in `training.py` is a proper VAE but operates on 256D float vectors, not text. They don't connect.

2. **The data pipeline is broken.** `ExperienceEncoder` produces 256D vectors from agent experiences, but that output is never wired into `FlumeVAETrainer`. The trainer falls back to synthetic Gaussian noise — N(0.5, 0.15). The VAE is learning the structure of random noise centered on HIHO=0.5.

3. **FLUME's only production use is cache similarity matching.** The 256D embeddings feed `SemanticCache` for fuzzy request deduplication. They don't guide agent actions, influence routing, or modulate execution. The investigation's vision of "reasoning latent space for agent control" doesn't exist in code.

4. **JourneyTracker doesn't use FLUME at all.** Its 12D trajectories come from SHA-256 hashing the task description text — pure deterministic text processing, no learned representations.

5. **No trained checkpoint exists.** `flume_vae_ep50.pt` isn't on disk. Production falls back to hash-based embeddings.

So "making the red flags green" isn't about running validation experiments on a working system — it's about **designing and building the system the investigation assumes already exists**.

## Job Title
Research Engineer, Universes

## Team Overview
The Universes team operates within Anthropic's Research division, focusing on developing AI models capable of handling sophisticated, extended-duration agentic tasks within realistic environments. The team designs novel training settings that transcend current model capabilities, enabling systems to navigate ambiguity, manage interruptions, sustain context through lengthy interactions, and make reasoned decisions in open-ended situations.

## Core Responsibilities

- Create thorough evaluation frameworks measuring genuine capability gains

- High agency and autonomy in work approach
- Strong software engineering capabilities enabling robust infrastructure development
- Demonstrable research judgment or senior technical expertise identifying what matters in complex domains

- Comfort navigating uncertainty with rapid adaptability
- Enthusiasm for pair programming collaboration

## Preferred Qualifications

- Background building reinforcement learning environments, simulation platforms, or large-scale ML infrastructure
- Senior-level expertise in relevant technical domains, even when transitioning fields
- Advanced knowledge of containerization, virtualization, sandboxing, or distributed systems
- Published influential research in relevant machine learning domains

## Research Focus Areas
The team emphasizes fundamental reinforcement learning research, training methodology innovation, and rigorous capability measurement. Anthropic's broader research directions include interpretability, scaling laws, multimodal systems, and AI safety.

## Compensation & Logistics
- Annual salary range: $500,000–$850,000 USD
- 25% minimum in-office requirement (hybrid model)
- Visa sponsorship available with dedicated immigration support
- Remote-friendly positions available in San Francisco, Seattle, or NYC

# FLUME VAE Architecture Design -- Fresh Start, Phased Approach

## 1. Diagnosis of Existing Problems

Before presenting the new design, here is a precise diagnosis of why the current system is broken, so the new architecture can avoid the same traps.

**Problem A: Two disconnected VAE implementations.**
- `autoencoder.py` (`FlumeEncoder`/`ThoughtEncoder`/`ThoughtDecoder`): A Transformer-based text-to-text autoencoder using a character-level tokenizer (`FlumeTokenizer`). It operates on token sequences and produces 256D latent vectors via mean-pooling. This is a deterministic autoencoder -- it has no `mu`/`logvar` split, no reparameterization trick, no KL divergence. It is NOT a VAE despite the codebase calling it one.
- `training.py` (`FlumeVAETrainer`): A proper MLP-based VAE that operates on raw 256D float vectors (not text). It has `mu_head`, `logvar_head`, reparameterization, and KL loss. But it trains on either random noise (synthetic gaussian centered at 0.5) or `ExperienceEncoder` output, which is a hand-crafted 256D vector composed of `[12D trajectory | 12 metrics | 5 one-hot | 227 SHA-256 hash expansion]`.

**Problem B: Neither VAE produces meaningful semantic embeddings.**
- The `FlumeVAETrainer` VAE encodes 256D vectors to 256D vectors -- it is an identity-dimension bottleneck. The "semantic fingerprint" segment (227 dims) is a deterministic SHA-256 hash expansion, which has zero semantic content. Training on this teaches the VAE to reconstruct hash noise, not learn semantic structure.
- The `FlumeEncoder` autoencoder uses a 74-character tokenizer. Even if trained, character-level encoding would produce poor semantic embeddings compared to subword tokenizers.

**Problem C: `vae_encoder.py` bridges these two systems incorrectly.**
- `FlumeVAEEncoder` loads the `FlumeVAETrainer` checkpoint (the MLP VAE), then feeds it SHA-256 hash-based 256D vectors as input. The "encoding" is: `text -> SHA-256 hash expand to 256D -> MLP encoder -> mu_head -> 256D output`. This means the VAE is just a learned nonlinear transformation of a hash -- it cannot capture semantic similarity because the input has no semantic information.

**Problem D: JourneyTracker's 12D trajectories are hash-based, not learned.**
- `text_to_latent` uses SHA-256 -> sine-wave expansion -> chunk-mean projection to get 12D coordinates. This is fully deterministic and has no semantic grounding. "Deploy API" and "API deployment" will produce completely different 12D points.

**Problem E: Training data is synthetic noise.**
- `FlumeTrajectoryDataset` falls back to `N(0.5, 0.15)` gaussian noise.
- `ExperienceDataset` encodes experiences using `ExperienceEncoder`, but 227/256 dimensions are SHA-256 hash bytes. The VAE learns to reconstruct hash patterns, not semantic structure.

## 2. New Architecture Design

### 2.1 Core Principle: Use Real Embeddings as Input

The fundamental fix is: **do not try to learn semantic embeddings from scratch with a small character-level model**. Instead, use a pre-trained embedding model (Ollama's `nomic-embed-text` which is already referenced in the codebase, or `snowflake-arctic-embed`) to produce high-quality input embeddings, then use the VAE to learn a compressed, structured latent space on top of those embeddings.

This is the architecture pattern used by successful VAE-based retrieval systems: pre-trained embeddings as input, VAE to learn structured latent space with KL regularization for smooth interpolation and generation.

### 2.2 Phase 1: Semantic Cache Engine

#### 2.2.1 Input Pipeline

```
Text Input
    |
    v
[Pre-trained Embedding Model]     <-- nomic-embed-text (768D) via Ollama
    |                                  or snowflake-arctic-embed (1024D)
    v
768D (or 1024D) dense embedding
    |
    v
[FLUME VAE Encoder]               <-- NEW: proper VAE
    |
    v
256D latent (mu vector used as embedding for cache)
```

**Why this approach:**
- Pre-trained embeddings already capture semantic similarity ("deploy API" ~ "API deployment")
- The VAE learns a *structured* 256D latent space where distances are meaningful
- KL regularization ensures the latent space is smooth (no "holes" between clusters)
- The decoder ensures the VAE retains information (reconstruction validates embedding quality)

#### 2.2.2 VAE Architecture

```
Encoder:
  Input:       D_in (768 for nomic-embed-text)
  Layer 1:     Linear(D_in, 512) + LayerNorm + GELU
  Layer 2:     Linear(512, 512) + LayerNorm + GELU
  Layer 3:     Linear(512, 384) + LayerNorm + GELU
  mu_head:     Linear(384, 256)
  logvar_head: Linear(384, 256)

  Reparameterization: z = mu + exp(0.5 * logvar) * eps,  eps ~ N(0, I)

Decoder:
  Input:       256 (latent z)
  Layer 1:     Linear(256, 384) + LayerNorm + GELU
  Layer 2:     Linear(384, 512) + LayerNorm + GELU
  Layer 3:     Linear(512, 512) + LayerNorm + GELU
  Output:      Linear(512, D_in)    <-- reconstruct original embedding
```

**Design choices:**
- **GELU over ReLU**: Smoother gradient landscape, standard in modern architectures
- **LayerNorm**: Stabilizes training, prevents internal covariate shift. Applied before activation.
- **Gradual dimension reduction**: 768 -> 512 -> 512 -> 384 -> 256 (no sudden bottleneck)
- **Symmetric decoder**: Mirror of encoder for balanced capacity
- **Output is reconstructed embedding, not text**: Reconstruction in embedding space is more tractable and directly validates latent quality

**Parameter count estimate:**
- Encoder: ~768*512 + 512*512 + 512*384 + 384*256 + 384*256 = ~1.1M parameters
- Decoder: ~256*384 + 384*512 + 512*512 + 512*768 = ~1.0M parameters
- Total: ~2.1M parameters (trainable on CPU in minutes)

#### 2.2.3 Loss Function

```python
L_total = L_recon + beta * L_KL + lambda_align * L_alignment + lambda_coh * L_coherence

Where:
  L_recon    = MSE(x_hat, x)                    # Reconstruct original embedding
  L_KL       = -0.5 * sum(1 + logvar - mu^2 - exp(logvar))  # KL to standard normal
  L_alignment = -mean(cos_sim(z_i, z_j) * label_ij)  # Contrastive alignment
  L_coherence = (mean(mu, dim=-1) - 0.5)^2      # HIHO coherence target (retained)
```

**KL annealing schedule (addresses Red Flag 1):**
```
beta(step) = min(beta_max, beta_min + (beta_max - beta_min) * step / warmup_steps)

Recommended:
  beta_min   = 0.0
  beta_max   = 0.1
  warmup_steps = 2000 (or 30% of total training steps)
```

**Free-bits strategy (addresses Red Flag 1):**
```
L_KL_free = sum(max(KL_j, free_bits_lambda))  for each latent dimension j

free_bits_lambda = 0.125 (bits)  -- minimum information per dimension
```

This prevents KL collapse where the encoder ignores the input and the decoder memorizes outputs. Each latent dimension must encode at least 0.125 bits.

**Contrastive alignment loss:**
```
For pairs (text_i, text_j) known to be semantically similar:
  L_alignment = -log(exp(cos(z_i, z_j)/tau) / sum_k(exp(cos(z_i, z_k)/tau)))
  tau = 0.07 (temperature)
```

This teaches the VAE to place similar texts nearby in latent space. Pairs come from:
- Paraphrases of the same task description
- Tasks with the same skill_name but different descriptions
- Augmented versions of the same text

#### 2.2.4 Training Data Strategy

**Bootstrap Data (Phase 1 start -- no real data yet):**

1. **Structured synthetic trajectories** (generated programmatically):
   - Generate 5,000 task descriptions across the 5 operation types (generate, analyze, search, transform, persist)
   - Template: `"{verb} {object} {qualifier}"` with semantic groups
   - Example groups: `["deploy API", "API deployment", "launch the API service"]` -> known-similar triples
   - Embed all with nomic-embed-text to get 768D vectors
   - This gives the VAE real semantic structure to learn from

2. **External datasets** (for breadth):
   - GSM8K problem descriptions (~8K samples): math reasoning task descriptions
   - Code docstrings from the Cohezion codebase itself (extract via AST parsing)
   - Vault experiment descriptions from `~/vaults/cohezion-vault/experiments/`

3. **Paraphrase mining** for contrastive pairs:
   - Use Ollama to generate 3 paraphrases per template task description
   - This gives ~15,000 positive pairs for contrastive alignment loss

**Fine-tune Data (accumulated over time):**

4. **Real execution data pipeline** (modify `ExperienceCollector`):
   - After each compound execution, store `(task_description, operation_type, metrics, skill_name)` as a JSONL record in `data/flume/experiences/`
   - Periodically re-embed all accumulated task descriptions with nomic-embed-text
   - Re-train VAE on the growing real dataset (weekly or on-demand)

**Data file format** (`data/flume/training/samples.jsonl`):
```json
{"text": "Deploy the API to production", "embedding": [0.1, ...768 floats...], "operation_type": "persist", "group_id": "deploy-api-1"}
{"text": "Launch API service in prod", "embedding": [0.12, ...768 floats...], "operation_type": "persist", "group_id": "deploy-api-1"}
```

The `group_id` field identifies semantic equivalence groups for contrastive loss.

#### 2.2.5 Training Pipeline

```
Step 1: Data Preparation
  - Load JSONL samples with pre-computed embeddings
  - If embeddings missing, batch-embed via Ollama nomic-embed-text
  - Split: 90% train, 10% validation
  - Build contrastive pairs from group_id

Step 2: Training Loop
  - Optimizer: AdamW(lr=1e-3, weight_decay=1e-4)
  - Scheduler: CosineAnnealingWarmRestarts(T_0=10, T_mult=2)
  - Gradient clipping: max_norm=1.0
  - Epochs: 100 (with early stopping on validation loss, patience=10)
  - KL annealing: linear warmup over first 30% of steps
  - Batch size: 128 (fits comfortably in CPU memory at 2.1M params)

Step 3: Checkpoint
  - Save best model (lowest validation loss)
  - Save at epoch 25, 50, 75, 100
  - Checkpoint format: {encoder, mu_head, logvar_head, decoder, optimizer, config, metrics}
  - Path: data/flume/checkpoints/flume_vae_v2_ep{N}.pt

Step 4: Evaluation (run automatically after training)
  - See Section 2.3 below
```

#### 2.2.6 Inference (Production Embedding)

```python
class FlumeVAEEncoder:
    def encode(self, text: str) -> np.ndarray:
        # Step 1: Get pre-trained embedding (768D)
        raw_embedding = self._get_pretrained_embedding(text)  # Ollama nomic-embed-text
        
        # Step 2: Pass through VAE encoder, take mu (deterministic at inference)
        with torch.no_grad():
            h = self.encoder(raw_embedding)
            mu = self.mu_head(h)
        
        # Step 3: L2-normalize for cosine similarity
        embedding = mu / (torch.norm(mu) + 1e-8)
        return embedding.numpy()
```

**Fallback chain:**
1. Ollama available + VAE checkpoint loaded -> Full FLUME embedding (best quality)
2. Ollama available + no VAE checkpoint -> Raw nomic-embed-text 768D (good quality, wrong dimension)
3. No Ollama + VAE checkpoint -> SHA-256 hash -> VAE encoder (poor quality, maintains dimension)
4. No Ollama + no VAE -> SHA-256 hash expansion (current fallback, worst quality)

### 2.3 Evaluation Framework (addresses Red Flags 3-5)

#### 2.3.1 Reconstruction Fidelity (Red Flag 2)

```
Metric: MSE between input embedding and reconstructed embedding
Target: MSE < 0.01 on validation set (normalized embeddings)

Test:
  For each sample in validation:
    z = encode(x)
    x_hat = decode(z)
    assert cosine_sim(x, x_hat) > 0.95
```

#### 2.3.2 Semantic Similarity Preservation (Red Flag 4)

```
Metric: Spearman correlation between:
  - Cosine similarity of original 768D embeddings (ground truth)
  - Cosine similarity of 256D FLUME latent vectors (learned)

Test:
  Sample 1000 text pairs, compute both similarity scores
  Assert Spearman rho > 0.90

Additional: STS-B benchmark correlation (if applicable)
```

#### 2.3.3 Paraphrase Discrimination (Phase 1 primary metric)

```
Metric: Precision@1 on paraphrase retrieval
  Given a query, is the nearest neighbor in latent space from the same group_id?

Test with known paraphrase groups:
  "deploy the API" should be closest to "launch API service"
  NOT closest to "analyze log data"

Target: Precision@1 > 0.85
```

#### 2.3.4 KL Health Monitoring (Red Flag 1)

```
Metrics to track per-epoch:
  - Total KL divergence (should stabilize, not collapse to 0)
  - Per-dimension KL (histogram: active vs inactive dimensions)
  - Active unit count: dimensions where KL_j > 0.01
  
Alerts:
  - Active units < 64 (of 256) -> KL collapse, increase free_bits
  - KL > 50 -> posterior collapse backward, reduce beta
  
Target: 128-200 active units (50-80% utilization)
```

#### 2.3.5 Cache Performance Ablation (Red Flag 5)

```
A/B test framework:
  A: SemanticCache with FLUME VAE embeddings (new)
  B: SemanticCache with SHA-256 hash embeddings (current)

Metrics:
  - Hit rate at threshold 0.88
  - True positive rate (semantically equivalent queries correctly matched)
  - False positive rate (unrelated queries incorrectly matched)
  - Precision-recall curve across thresholds [0.70, 0.75, 0.80, 0.85, 0.90, 0.95]

Run on:
  - Synthetic benchmark: 200 query groups with known paraphrases
  - Production replay: record real cache queries for 1 week, replay against both systems
```

#### 2.4.2 JourneyTracker Integration (Phase 2 prep)

File: `/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/src/cohezion/compound/journey_tracker.py`

**Phase 1 change (minimal):** Add an optional FLUME embedding path alongside the existing hash-based path:

```python
def text_to_latent(self, text: str) -> np.ndarray:
    """Generate 2048D embedding from text.
    Phase 1: Optionally use FLUME VAE for first 256D, hash-expand rest.
    Phase 2: Full FLUME VAE -> learned projection to 12D.
    """
    if self._flume_encoder is not None:
        # Use FLUME VAE for semantic grounding
        flume_256d = self._flume_encoder.encode(text)  # 256D
        # Tile/project to 2048D for compatibility with existing holographic_project
        latent = np.tile(flume_256d, 8)  # 256 * 8 = 2048
    else:
        # Existing hash-based path (fallback)
        latent = self._hash_text_to_latent(text)
    return latent
```

This provides semantic grounding while maintaining the existing 12D projection pipeline. The holographic projection (chunk-mean averaging) will now operate on semantically meaningful vectors instead of hash noise.

#### 2.4.3 ExperienceEncoder Integration

File: `/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/src/cohezion/flume/experience_encoder.py`

**Phase 1 change:** Replace the 227D SHA-256 fingerprint segment with FLUME VAE output (truncated/projected to 227D):

Current layout: `[12D trajectory | 12 metrics | 5 op_type | 227 SHA-256 fingerprint]`
New layout:     `[12D trajectory | 12 metrics | 5 op_type | 227 FLUME semantic (projected from 256D)]`

The projection from 256D to 227D is a simple learned linear layer or PCA projection, trained alongside or after the VAE.

### 2.5 Phase 2: Reasoning Latent Space

#### 2.5.1 What Changes from Phase 1

| Component | Phase 1 | Phase 2 |
|-----------|---------|---------|
| **VAE input** | Pre-trained text embeddings (768D) | Reasoning trajectory sequences (variable length) |
| **Encoder** | MLP (768 -> 256) | Temporal encoder: LSTM/Transformer over trajectory steps |
| **Decoder** | MLP (256 -> 768) | Temporal decoder: autoregressive trajectory generation |
| **Latent use** | Similarity matching (cosine distance) | Navigation, interpolation, sampling |
| **Training data** | Text descriptions + paraphrases | Execution trajectories from compound loop |
| **Loss** | MSE + KL + contrastive | MSE + KL + trajectory smoothness + HIHO coherence |

#### 2.5.2 Phase 2 Encoder Architecture

```
Input: Sequence of trajectory steps [(12D_state, metrics, op_type), ...]
       Each step encoded as 29D (12 + 12 + 5) by ExperienceEncoder prefix

Temporal Encoder:
  Step embedding: Linear(29, 128) + GELU
  Positional encoding: Sinusoidal (max_len=100 steps)
  Transformer: 2 layers, 4 heads, d_model=128, d_ff=256
  Pooling: Attention-weighted mean over sequence
  Project: Linear(128, 256) -> mu_head(256), logvar_head(256)

Temporal Decoder:
  Input: z (256D) + step position
  Project: Linear(256, 128)
  Transformer decoder: 2 layers, 4 heads, d_model=128, d_ff=256
  Output: Linear(128, 29) per step -> reconstructed trajectory
```

#### 2.5.3 Phase 2 Loss Function

```
L_total = L_recon_traj + beta * L_KL + lambda_smooth * L_smoothness + lambda_coh * L_coherence

Where:
  L_recon_traj  = MSE between input trajectory steps and reconstructed steps
  L_smoothness  = mean(||z_{t+1} - z_t||^2)  over consecutive latent encodings
  L_coherence   = (mean(mu) - 0.5)^2          HIHO target
```

#### 2.5.4 Latent Space Operations (Phase 2)

**Interpolation:** Given trajectories A and B:
```python
z_A = encoder(trajectory_A)
z_B = encoder(trajectory_B)
z_interp = (1 - alpha) * z_A + alpha * z_B
trajectory_interp = decoder(z_interp)  # Novel reasoning path
```

**Sampling:** Generate novel trajectories:
```python
z_sample = torch.randn(256)  # Sample from prior
trajectory_novel = decoder(z_sample)
```

**Navigation:** Move in latent space to modify reasoning strategy:
```python
z_current = encoder(current_trajectory)
direction = z_target_style - z_current_style  # "make it more analytical"
z_guided = z_current + alpha * direction
new_trajectory = decoder(z_guided)
```

#### 2.5.5 JourneyTracker Full Integration (Phase 2)

Replace `text_to_latent` + `holographic_project` entirely:
```python
def compute_trajectory_embedding(self, trajectory_points: list[TrajectoryPoint]) -> np.ndarray:
    """Encode full trajectory into 256D FLUME latent vector."""
    step_features = [self._encode_step(p) for p in trajectory_points]
    z = self.flume_vae.encode_trajectory(step_features)  # 256D
    return z

def project_to_12d(self, z_256d: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
    """Learned projection from 256D latent to 12D axiomatic space."""
    return self.projection_head(z_256d)  # Linear(256, 12) trained end-to-end
```

The `projection_head` (Linear 256->12) is trained as part of the Phase 2 VAE with an auxiliary loss that the 12D output should match the axiomatic dimension semantics (novelty, logic, field, spatial, temporal, precipitation, coherence, efficiency, convergence, smoothness, resonance, harmony).

| 3 | Build Phase 2 temporal encoder alongside Phase 1 MLP encoder | Low -- additive |
| 4 | Train Phase 2 on accumulated data | Low -- offline training |
| 5 | Validate Phase 2 latent space quality (interpolation, sampling) | Medium -- new capability |

| 7 | Connect MorphospaceMapper to Phase 2 latent space | Medium -- navigation changes |
| 8 | A/B test Phase 1 vs Phase 2 for cache performance | Low -- comparison only |

**What stays across phases:**
- 256D latent dimension
- KL + reconstruction loss structure
- Free-bits strategy
- HIHO coherence regularization
- Checkpoint format (extended, not replaced)
- `FlumeVAEEncoder` public interface (`encode(text) -> 256D`)
- SemanticCache integration (same `EmbeddingModel` interface)
- Evaluation framework (extended with trajectory-specific metrics)

**What changes:**
- Encoder architecture (MLP -> Temporal Transformer)
- Input modality (text embeddings -> trajectory sequences)
- Decoder output (reconstructed embedding -> reconstructed trajectory)
- Additional loss terms (smoothness, trajectory coherence)
- JourneyTracker integration (shallow -> deep)

### 2.9 Potential Challenges

1. **Ollama availability**: The system must gracefully degrade when Ollama is not running. The `EmbeddingProvider` abstraction handles this with a fallback chain. Tests must mock the Ollama dependency.

2. **Training data cold start**: With zero real execution data, the initial model quality depends entirely on synthetic task descriptions. The mitigation is to generate high-quality synthetic data with known paraphrase groups, and to begin accumulating real data immediately.

3. **Checkpoint migration**: Existing checkpoints from `FlumeVAETrainer` are incompatible with the new `FlumeVAE`. The `vae_encoder.py` must detect checkpoint version and load accordingly. A version field in the checkpoint dict handles this.

4. **Performance at inference**: Each `encode()` call requires Ollama embedding (network call) + VAE forward pass (CPU). For the cache use case, this happens once per query. Latency target: <50ms total (Ollama ~30ms for nomic-embed-text, VAE forward ~1ms on CPU). Caching the Ollama embedding per-text can further reduce this.

5. **KL collapse despite free-bits**: If the reconstruction task is too easy (768D -> 256D is not a severe bottleneck), the KL may still collapse. Monitor active units aggressively. If collapse occurs, options: reduce decoder capacity, increase beta, or add a secondary task (contrastive loss provides this naturally).

It also maps FLUME's capabilities to the Anthropic Universes team Research Engineer role to validate strategic alignment.\n\n---\n\n## Red Flag → Green Light Summary\n\n| # | Red Flag | Root Cause | Green Light Criteria | Phase |\n|---|----------|-----------|---------------------|-------|\n| 1 | KL collapse risk | No KL annealing, trains on noise | KL > 0.1, 128+ active latent units (of 256) | 1 |\n| 2 | Reconstruction fidelity unknown | Two VAEs, neither validated | cos_sim(input, reconstructed) > 0.95 on validation set | 1 |\n| 3 | Synthetic quality unknown | No generation/evaluation pipeline | Paraphrase Precision@1 > 0.85, Spearman ρ > 0.90 | 1 |\n| 4 | Evaluation metrics unvalidated | JourneyTracker uses SHA-256 hashes | Metrics correlate with task performance R² > 0.5 | 1+2 |\n| 5 | Integration impact unknown | FLUME only used for cache similarity | A/B: FLUME cache outperforms hash on precision-recall | 1+2 |\n| 6 | Universes team fit unclear | No mapping to their research needs | Clear alignment document with 3+ intersection points | 1 |\n\n---\n\n## Architecture: Fresh FLUME VAE\n\n### Core Insight\n\nDon't learn semantic embeddings from scratch with a tiny model. Use a **pre-trained embedding model** (Ollama `nomic-embed-text`, 768D) as input, then train a **proper VAE** to learn a structured, compressed 256D latent space on top of those embeddings.\n\n### Phase 1 Architecture: Semantic Cache Engine\n\n```\nText Input\n    │\n    ▼\n[Ollama nomic-embed-text]  ─── 768D dense semantic embedding\n    │\n    ▼\n[FLUME VAE Encoder]         ─── MLP: 768→512→512→384 → mu(256), logvar(256)\n    │\n    ▼\n256D latent z (mu used as embedding at inference)\n    │\n    ▼\n[FLUME VAE Decoder]         ─── MLP: 256→384→512→512→768 (reconstruct input)\n    │\n    ▼\n[SemanticCache]             ─── cosine similarity matching (threshold ~0.88)\n```\n\n**Why this works:**\n- Pre-trained embeddings already capture \"deploy API\" ≈ \"API deployment\"\n- VAE learns *structured* 256D space where distances are meaningful\n- KL regularization ensures smooth latent space (no holes between clusters)\n- Decoder forces VAE to retain information (reconstruction validates quality)\n- ~2.1M parameters — trains on CPU in minutes\n\n### Loss Function\n\n```\nL = L_recon + β·L_KL + λ_align·L_contrastive + λ_coh·L_coherence\n\nL_recon       = MSE(decoded, original_embedding)\nL_KL          = -0.5·Σ(1 + logvar - μ² - exp(logvar))    with free-bits per dimension\nL_contrastive = InfoNCE over known paraphrase pairs        (τ=0.07)\nL_coherence   = (mean(μ) - 0.5)²                          HIHO target\n```\n\n**KL collapse prevention (Red Flag 1):**\n- β annealing: 0.0 → 0.1 over first 30% of training steps\n- Free-bits: min 0.125 bits/dimension — forces each dimension to encode information\n- Active unit monitoring: alert if <128 of 256 dimensions are active\n\n### Phase 2 Architecture: Reasoning Latent Space\n\nExtends Phase 1 with a **temporal encoder** over trajectory sequences:\n\n```\nTrajectory Steps [(12D_state, metrics, op_type), ...]\n    │\n    ▼\n[Step Embedding]       ─── Linear(29→128) + positional encoding\n    │\n    ▼\n[Transformer Encoder]  ─── 2 layers, 4 heads, d_model=128\n    │\n    ▼\n[Attention Pooling → mu(256), logvar(256)]\n    │\n    ▼\n[Transformer Decoder]  ─── autoregressive trajectory reconstruction\n```\n\nEnables: interpolation between strategies, novel trajectory sampling, latent space navigation for agent guidance. JourneyTracker transitions from SHA-256 hashing to learned FLUME projections.\n\n---\n\n## Training Data Strategy (Hybrid)\n\n### Bootstrap (Phase 1 — immediate)\n\n1. **Structured synthetic tasks** (~5,000 samples)\n   - Task descriptions across 5 operation types (generate, analyze, search, transform, persist)\n   - Template groups with known paraphrases: `[\"deploy API\", \"API deployment\", \"launch API service\"]`\n   - Each embedded via `nomic-embed-text` → 768D vectors\n\n2. **Codebase docstrings** (~2,000 samples)\n   - Extract from Cohezion source via AST parsing\n   - Real domain-specific language\n\n3. **Vault experiment descriptions** (variable, ~500+ samples)\n   - From `~/vaults/cohezion-vault/experiments/*.json`\n\n4. **Paraphrase generation** (~15,000 pairs)\n   - Use Ollama to generate 3 paraphrases per task template\n   - Provides contrastive training pairs\n\n### Fine-tune (accumulates over time)\n\n5. **Real execution data pipeline**\n   - After each compound execution: store `(task_description, operation_type, metrics, skill_name)` as JSONL\n   - Path: `data/flume/experiences/execution_log.jsonl`\n   - Periodic re-embed + re-train (weekly or on-demand)\n\n### External (Phase 2)\n\n6. **Published reasoning datasets** (GSM8K chains, MATH solutions, code generation traces)\n   - For trajectory-level training in Phase 2\n\n---\n\n## Universes Team Alignment (Red Flag 6)\n\n**Source:** [Research Engineer, Universes](https://job-boards.greenhouse.io/anthropic/jobs/5061517008)\n\n### Mapping: Team Needs → FLUME Capabilities\n\n| Universes Team Need | FLUME Capability | Alignment |\n|---------------------|------------------|-----------|\n| **Agentic training environments** for extended-duration tasks | Compound engineering loop: 11-step execution pipeline with skill refinement, retrospection, and journey tracking. Literally an RL training environment for agentic tasks. | **Strong** |\n| **Evaluation frameworks measuring genuine capability gains** | Red Flag 4 resolution produces a validated evaluation framework with correlation-tested metrics. Phase 1 evaluation suite (reconstruction fidelity, similarity preservation, KL health, cache ablation) demonstrates evaluation engineering rigor. | **Strong** |\n| **Navigate ambiguity, manage interruptions, sustain context** | Phase 2 reasoning latent space compresses reasoning trajectories for context sustainability. 256D embeddings preserve semantic meaning across long interactions. Morphospace navigation handles ambiguity via stability wells. | **Strong** (Phase 2) |\n| **RL environments and simulation platforms** | 12D universe simulation with FLUME VAE as the representation layer. JourneyTracker records state transitions. DegradationDetector monitors drift. This is RL infrastructure. | **Strong** |\n| **Integrate into production training systems** | Phase 1 semantic cache engine is a production system. Demonstrates ability to ship ML infrastructure, not just research prototypes. | **Strong** |\n| **Rapidly troubleshoot across research and production ML** | This spec itself — diagnosing architectural fractures, designing fixes, implementing with TDD — demonstrates this capability directly. | **Meta** |\n\n### Preferred Qualifications Match\n\n| Qualification | Evidence |\n|---------------|----------|\n| LLM training/fine-tuning/evaluation | VAE training pipeline, evaluation framework, metric validation |\n| RL environments, simulation platforms | 12D universe simulation, compound loop as RL env |\n| Large-scale ML infrastructure | SemanticCache, ExperienceCollector, multi-tier data pipeline |\n| Containerization, distributed systems | Cloud Run deployment, Tailscale Funnel for MCP, multi-session worktree pattern |\n\n### Positioning Document (Deliverable)\n\nProduce a concise document (max 2 pages) that:\n1. Frames FLUME as an **agentic training environment** with validated evaluation\n2. Highlights the **evaluation engineering** (not just the model)\n3. Shows **production readiness** (Phase 1 cache engine in production)\n4. Positions the **compound engineering loop** as an RL training methodology\n5. Demonstrates research taste: honest red-flag identification, systematic resolution\n\n---\n\n## Implementation Plan\n\n### Phase 1 Scope (This Spec)\n\n**New files:**\n\n| File | Purpose | ~Lines |\n|------|---------|--------|\n| `src/cohezion/flume/vae.py` | Core VAE model (encoder, decoder, reparameterization, loss) | 200 |\n| `src/cohezion/flume/embedding_provider.py` | Pre-trained embedding interface (Ollama + hash fallback) | 120 |\n| `src/cohezion/flume/train_vae.py` | Training pipeline (KL annealing, free-bits, contrastive) | 250 |\n| `src/cohezion/flume/evaluate_vae.py` | Evaluation framework (all red flag metrics) | 200 |\n| `src/cohezion/flume/data_pipeline.py` | Data prep (synthetic tasks, embedding, contrastive pairs) | 180 |\n| `scripts/train_flume_v2.py` | CLI training entry point | 50 |\n| `tests/flume/test_vae_v2.py` | VAE model unit tests | 150 |\n| `tests/flume/test_embedding_provider.py` | Embedding provider tests | 100 |\n| `tests/flume/test_train_vae_v2.py` | Training pipeline tests | 150 |\n| `tests/flume/test_evaluate_vae.py` | Evaluation framework tests | 120 |\n| `tests/flume/test_data_pipeline.py` | Data pipeline tests | 100 |\n\n**Modified files:**\n\n| File | Change | Risk |\n|------|--------|------|\n| `src/cohezion/flume/vae_encoder.py` | Load new VAE model, use EmbeddingProvider. Public interface unchanged. | Low |\n| `src/cohezion/flume/__init__.py` | Add new exports | Low |\n| `src/cohezion/compound/journey_tracker.py` | Optional FLUME encoder path in `text_to_latent()` | Low |\n| `src/cohezion/swarm/semantic_cache.py` | Threshold validation after new model (may need tuning) | Low |\n\n**Deprecated (not deleted):**\n\n| File | Reason |\n|------|--------|\n| `src/cohezion/flume/training.py` | Replaced by `train_vae.py` |\n| `src/cohezion/flume/dataset.py` | Replaced by `data_pipeline.py` |\n\n### Task Breakdown\n\n**Sprint 1: Foundation**\n\n- [ ] Task 1: Create `embedding_provider.py` — Ollama nomic-embed-text wrapper with hash fallback chain\n- [ ] Task 2: Create `vae.py` — FlumeVAE model (encoder, decoder, reparameterization, configurable loss)\n- [ ] Task 3: Create `data_pipeline.py` — synthetic task generation, batch embedding, contrastive pair mining\n- [ ] Task 4: Tests for Tasks 1-3\n\n**Sprint 2: Training**\n\n- [ ] Task 5: Create `train_vae.py` — full training loop with KL annealing, free-bits, contrastive loss, checkpointing\n- [ ] Task 6: Create `scripts/train_flume_v2.py` — CLI entry point\n- [ ] Task 7: Generate bootstrap training data (synthetic tasks + codebase docstrings + vault experiments)\n- [ ] Task 8: Train initial model, validate convergence (loss curves, KL health)\n\n**Sprint 3: Evaluation + Integration**\n\n- [ ] Task 9: Create `evaluate_vae.py` — reconstruction fidelity, similarity preservation, paraphrase discrimination, KL health, cache ablation\n- [ ] Task 10: Run evaluation suite, validate all metrics meet green-light criteria\n- [ ] Task 11: Update `vae_encoder.py` to load new VAE (keep public interface identical)\n- [ ] Task 12: Update `journey_tracker.py` with optional FLUME path\n- [ ] Task 13: Run SemanticCache A/B: FLUME VAE embeddings vs. hash embeddings\n\n**Sprint 4: Data Collection + Positioning**\n\n- [ ] Task 14: Instrument `ExperienceCollector` to log task descriptions for future training\n- [ ] Task 15: Write Universes team positioning document (map FLUME to job description)\n- [ ] Task 16: End-to-end integration test: compound execution → data collection → embedding → cache hit\n\n### Key Integration Points\n\n**`vae_encoder.py`** (primary integration surface):\n```\nCurrent: text → SHA-256 hash → expand to 256D → MLP encoder → mu → 256D\nNew:     text → Ollama embed → 768D → VAE encoder → mu → 256D\n         (fallback: text → SHA-256 hash → expand to 256D if Ollama unavailable)\n```\n\n**`semantic_cache.py`** (consumer — no interface changes):\n```\nFlumeVAEEmbeddingModel.encode(text) → EmbeddingResult(embedding=[...256 floats...])\n# Same interface, better embeddings\n```\n\n**`journey_tracker.py`** (Phase 1 minimal change):\n```\ntext_to_latent(text):\n    if flume_encoder available:\n        flume_256d = flume_encoder.encode(text)\n        return np.tile(flume_256d, 8)  # 256×8 = 2048D for existing pipeline\n    else:\n        return hash_based_latent(text)  # existing behavior\n```\n\n---\n\n## Verification Strategy\n\n### Per Red Flag\n\n| Red Flag | Verification Command/Method | Pass Criteria |\n|----------|----------------------------|---------------|\n| 1. KL collapse | `uv run python -m scripts.train_flume_v2 --evaluate-kl` | Active units ≥ 128/256, KL > 0.1 |\n| 2. Reconstruction | `uv run pytest tests/flume/test_evaluate_vae.py -k reconstruction` | cos_sim(input, recon) > 0.95 on validation |\n| 3. Synthetic quality | `uv run pytest tests/flume/test_evaluate_vae.py -k paraphrase` | Precision@1 > 0.85 |\n| 4. Metrics validation | `uv run pytest tests/flume/test_evaluate_vae.py -k similarity_preservation` | Spearman ρ > 0.90 between original and latent similarities |\n| 5. Integration impact | `uv run pytest tests/flume/test_evaluate_vae.py -k cache_ablation` | FLUME precision-recall dominates hash baseline |\n| 6. Team fit | Read positioning document, verify 3+ alignment points | Qualitative: convincing narrative |\n\n### Full Suite\n\n```bash\n# Unit tests\nuv run pytest tests/flume/ -v\n\n# Train and evaluate\nuv run python scripts/train_flume_v2.py --config default --evaluate\n\n# Integration test\nuv run pytest tests/flume/test_evaluate_vae.py -v\n\n# Existing test suite (no regressions)\nuv run pytest tests/ -q\n```\n\n### Go/No-Go Gate (Phase 1 → Phase 2)\n\nPhase 2 proceeds only if ALL Phase 1 green-light criteria are met:\n- [ ] KL healthy (128+ active units)\n- [ ] Reconstruction fidelity (cos_sim > 0.95)\n- [ ] Paraphrase discrimination (P@1 > 0.85)\n- [ ] Similarity preservation (ρ > 0.90)\n- [ ] Cache A/B positive (FLUME beats hash)\n- [ ] Real execution data collection pipeline operational\n"

KL collapse | `uv run python -m scripts.train_flume_v2 --evaluate-kl` | Active units ≥ 128/256, KL > 0.1 |\n| 2. Reconstruction | `uv run pytest tests/flume/test_evaluate_vae.py -k reconstruction` | cos_sim(input, recon) > 0.95 on validation |\n| 3. Synthetic quality | `uv run pytest tests/flume/test_evaluate_vae.py -k paraphrase` | Precision@1 > 0.85 |\n| 4. Metrics validation | `uv run pytest tests/flume/test_evaluate_vae.py -k similarity_preservation` | Spearman ρ > 0.90 between original and latent similarities |\n| 5. Integration impact | `uv run pytest tests/flume/test_evaluate_vae.py -k cache_ablation` | FLUME precision-recall dominates hash baseline |\n| 6. Team fit | Read positioning document, verify 3+ alignment points | Qualitative: convincing narrative |\n\n### Full Suite\n\n```bash\n# Unit tests\nuv run pytest tests/flume/ -v\n\n# Train and evaluate\nuv run python scripts/train_flume_v2.py --config default --evaluate\n\n# Integration test\nuv run pytest tests/flume/test_evaluate_vae.py -v\n\n# Existing test suite (no regressions)\nuv run pytest tests/ -q\n```\n\n### Go/No-Go Gate (Phase 1 → Phase 2)\n\nPhase 2 proceeds only if ALL Phase 1 green-light criteria are met:\n- [ ] KL healthy (128+ active units)\n- [ ] Reconstruction fidelity (cos_sim > 0.95)\n- [ ] Paraphrase discrimination (P@1 > 0.85)\n- [ ] Similarity preservation (ρ > 0.90)\n- [ ] Cache A/B positive (FLUME beats hash)\n- [ ] Real execution data collection pipeline operational\n"
}

     1→# FLUME VAE: Red Flags → Green Lights
     2→
     3→## Context
     4→
     5→The FLUME investigation (2026-02-23) identified 6 red flags — critical unknowns that determine whether FLUME is a viable research artifact or an architectural dead end. Codebase exploration revealed the situation is worse than the investigation assumed: **the system the investigation analyzed doesn't actually exist.** Two incompatible VAE implementations, a broken data pipeline training on random noise, and zero integration with agent behavior mean the red flags aren't "unknowns to measure" — they're **architectural problems to solve.**
     6→
     7→This plan rebuilds FLUME from first principles in two phases, turning each red flag green with concrete, verifiable criteria. It also maps FLUME's capabilities to the Anthropic Universes team Research Engineer role to validate strategic alignment.
     8→
     9→---
    10→
    11→## Red Flag → Green Light Summary
    12→
    13→| # | Red Flag | Root Cause | Green Light Criteria | Phase |
    14→|---|----------|-----------|---------------------|-------|
    15→| 1 | KL collapse risk | No KL annealing, trains on noise | KL > 0.1, 128+ active latent units (of 256) | 1 |
    16→| 2 | Reconstruction fidelity unknown | Two VAEs, neither validated | cos_sim(input, reconstructed) > 0.95 on validation set | 1 |
    17→| 3 | Synthetic quality unknown | No generation/evaluation pipeline | Paraphrase Precision@1 > 0.85, Spearman ρ > 0.90 | 1 |
    18→| 4 | Evaluation metrics unvalidated | JourneyTracker uses SHA-256 hashes | Metrics correlate with task performance R² > 0.5 | 1+2 |
    19→| 5 | Integration impact unknown | FLUME only used for cache similarity | A/B: FLUME cache outperforms hash on precision-recall | 1+2 |
    20→| 6 | Universes team fit unclear | No mapping to their research needs | Clear alignment document with 3+ intersection points | 1 |
    21→
    22→---
    23→
    24→## Architecture: Fresh FLUME VAE
    25→
    26→### Core Insight
    27→
    28→Don't learn semantic embeddings from scratch with a tiny model. Use a **pre-trained embedding model** (Ollama `nomic-embed-text`, 768D) as input, then train a **proper VAE** to learn a structured, compressed 256D latent space on top of those embeddings.
    29→
    30→### Phase 1 Architecture: Semantic Cache Engine
    31→
    32→```
    33→Text Input
    34→    │
    35→    ▼
    36→[Ollama nomic-embed-text]  ─── 768D dense semantic embedding
    37→    │
    38→    ▼
    39→[FLUME VAE Encoder]         ─── MLP: 768→512→512→384 → mu(256), logvar(256)
    40→    │
    41→    ▼
    42→256D latent z (mu used as embedding at inference)
    43→    │
    44→    ▼
    45→[FLUME VAE Decoder]         ─── MLP: 256→384→512→512→768 (reconstruct input)
    46→    │
    47→    ▼
    48→[SemanticCache]             ─── cosine similarity matching (threshold ~0.88)
    49→```
    50→
    51→**Why this works:**
    52→- Pre-trained embeddings already capture "deploy API" ≈ "API deployment"
    53→- VAE learns *structured* 256D space where distances are meaningful
    54→- KL regularization ensures smooth latent space (no holes between clusters)
    55→- Decoder forces VAE to retain information (reconstruction validates quality)
    56→- ~2.1M parameters — trains on CPU in minutes
    57→
    58→### Loss Function
    59→
    60→```
    61→L = L_recon + β·L_KL + λ_align·L_contrastive + λ_coh·L_coherence
    62→
    63→L_recon       = MSE(decoded, original_embedding)
    64→L_KL          = -0.5·Σ(1 + logvar - μ² - exp(logvar))    with free-bits per dimension
    65→L_contrastive = InfoNCE over known paraphrase pairs        (τ=0.07)
    66→L_coherence   = (mean(μ) - 0.5)²                          HIHO target
    67→```
    68→
    69→**KL collapse prevention (Red Flag 1):**
    70→- β annealing: 0.0 → 0.1 over first 30% of training steps
    71→- Free-bits: min 0.125 bits/dimension — forces each dimension to encode information
    72→- Active unit monitoring: alert if <128 of 256 dimensions are active
    73→
    74→### Phase 2 Architecture: Reasoning Latent Space
    75→
    76→Extends Phase 1 with a **temporal encoder** over trajectory sequences:
    77→
    78→```
    79→Trajectory Steps [(12D_state, metrics, op_type), ...]
    80→    │
    81→    ▼
    82→[Step Embedding]       ─── Linear(29→128) + positional encoding
    83→    │
    84→    ▼
    85→[Transformer Encoder]  ─── 2 layers, 4 heads, d_model=128
    86→    │
    87→    ▼
    88→[Attention Pooling → mu(256), logvar(256)]
    89→    │
    90→    ▼
    91→[Transformer Decoder]  ─── autoregressive trajectory reconstruction
    92→```
    93→
    94→Enables: interpolation between strategies, novel trajectory sampling, latent space navigation for agent guidance. JourneyTracker transitions from SHA-256 hashing to learned FLUME projections.
    95→
    96→---
    97→
    98→## Training Data Strategy (Hybrid)
    99→
   100→### Bootstrap (Phase 1 — immediate)
   101→
   102→1. **Structured synthetic tasks** (~5,000 samples)
   103→   - Task descriptions across 5 operation types (generate, analyze, search, transform, persist)
   104→   - Template groups with known paraphrases: `["deploy API", "API deployment", "launch API service"]`
   105→   - Each embedded via `nomic-embed-text` → 768D vectors
   106→
   107→2. **Codebase docstrings** (~2,000 samples)
   108→   - Extract from Cohezion source via AST parsing
   109→   - Real domain-specific language
   110→
   111→3. **Vault experiment descriptions** (variable, ~500+ samples)
   112→   - From `~/vaults/cohezion-vault/experiments/*.json`
   113→
   114→4. **Paraphrase generation** (~15,000 pairs)
   115→   - Use Ollama to generate 3 paraphrases per task template
   116→   - Provides contrastive training pairs
   117→
   118→### Fine-tune (accumulates over time)
   119→
   120→5. **Real execution data pipeline**
   121→   - After each compound execution: store `(task_description, operation_type, metrics, skill_name)` as JSONL
   122→   - Path: `data/flume/experiences/execution_log.jsonl`
   123→   - Periodic re-embed + re-train (weekly or on-demand)
   124→
   125→### External (Phase 2)
   126→
   127→6. **Published reasoning datasets** (GSM8K chains, MATH solutions, code generation traces)
   128→   - For trajectory-level training in Phase 2
   129→
   130→---
   131→
   132→## Universes Team Alignment (Red Flag 6)
   133→
   134→**Source:** [Research Engineer, Universes](https://job-boards.greenhouse.io/anthropic/jobs/5061517008)
   135→
   136→### Mapping: Team Needs → FLUME Capabilities
   137→
   138→| Universes Team Need | FLUME Capability | Alignment |
   139→|---------------------|------------------|-----------|
   140→| **Agentic training environments** for extended-duration tasks | Compound engineering loop: 11-step execution pipeline with skill refinement, retrospection, and journey tracking. Literally an RL training environment for agentic tasks. | **Strong** |
   141→| **Evaluation frameworks measuring genuine capability gains** | Red Flag 4 resolution produces a validated evaluation framework with correlation-tested metrics. Phase 1 evaluation suite (reconstruction fidelity, similarity preservation, KL health, cache ablation) demonstrates evaluation engineering rigor. | **Strong** |
   142→| **Navigate ambiguity, manage interruptions, sustain context** | Phase 2 reasoning latent space compresses reasoning trajectories for context sustainability. 256D embeddings preserve semantic meaning across long interactions. Morphospace navigation handles ambiguity via stability wells. | **Strong** (Phase 2) |
   143→| **RL environments and simulation platforms** | 12D universe simulation with FLUME VAE as the representation layer. JourneyTracker records state transitions. DegradationDetector monitors drift. This is RL infrastructure. | **Strong** |
   144→| **Integrate into production training systems** | Phase 1 semantic cache engine is a production system. Demonstrates ability to ship ML infrastructure, not just research prototypes. | **Strong** |
   145→| **Rapidly troubleshoot across research and production ML** | This spec itself — diagnosing architectural fractures, designing fixes, implementing with TDD — demonstrates this capability directly. | **Meta** |
   146→
   147→### Preferred Qualifications Match
   148→
   149→| Qualification | Evidence |
   150→|---------------|----------|
   151→| LLM training/fine-tuning/evaluation | VAE training pipeline, evaluation framework, metric validation |
   152→| RL environments, simulation platforms | 12D universe simulation, compound loop as RL env |
   153→| Large-scale ML infrastructure | SemanticCache, ExperienceCollector, multi-tier data pipeline |
   154→| Containerization, distributed systems | Cloud Run deployment, Tailscale Funnel for MCP, multi-session worktree pattern |
   155→
   156→### Positioning Document (Deliverable)
   157→
   158→Produce a concise document (max 2 pages) that:
   159→1. Frames FLUME as an **agentic training environment** with validated evaluation
   160→2. Highlights the **evaluation engineering** (not just the model)
   161→3. Shows **production readiness** (Phase 1 cache engine in production)
   162→4. Positions the **compound engineering loop** as an RL training methodology
   163→5. Demonstrates research taste: honest red-flag identification, systematic resolution
   164→

   204→

   233→### Key Integration Points
   234→
   235→**`vae_encoder.py`** (primary integration surface):
   236→```
   237→Current: text → SHA-256 hash → expand to 256D → MLP encoder → mu → 256D
   238→New:     text → Ollama embed → 768D → VAE encoder → mu → 256D
   239→         (fallback: text → SHA-256 hash → expand to 256D if Ollama unavailable)
   240→```
   241→
   242→**`semantic_cache.py`** (consumer — no interface changes):
   243→```
   244→FlumeVAEEmbeddingModel.encode(text) → EmbeddingResult(embedding=[...256 floats...])
   245→# Same interface, better embeddings
   246→```
   247→
   248→**`journey_tracker.py`** (Phase 1 minimal change):
   249→```
   250→text_to_latent(text):
   251→    if flume_encoder available:
   252→        flume_256d = flume_encoder.encode(text)
   253→        return np.tile(flume_256d, 8)  # 256×8 = 2048D for existing pipeline
   254→    else:
   255→        return hash_based_latent(text)  # existing behavior
   256→```

   260→## Verification Strategy
   261→
   262→### Per Red Flag
   263→
   264→| Red Flag | Verification Command/Method | Pass Criteria |
   265→|----------|----------------------------|---------------|
   266→| 1. KL collapse | `uv run python -m scripts.train_flume_v2 --evaluate-kl` | Active units ≥ 128/256, KL > 0.1 |
   267→| 2. Reconstruction | `uv run pytest tests/flume/test_evaluate_vae.py -k reconstruction` | cos_sim(input, recon) > 0.95 on validation |
   268→| 3. Synthetic quality | `uv run pytest tests/flume/test_evaluate_vae.py -k paraphrase` | Precision@1 > 0.85 |
   269→| 4. Metrics validation | `uv run pytest tests/flume/test_evaluate_vae.py -k similarity_preservation` | Spearman ρ > 0.90 between original and latent similarities |
   270→| 5. Integration impact | `uv run pytest tests/flume/test_evaluate_vae.py -k cache_ablation` | FLUME precision-recall dominates hash baseline |
   271→| 6. Team fit | Read positioning document, verify 3+ alignment points | Qualitative: convincing narrative |
   272→
   273→### Full Suite
   274→
   275→```bash
   276→# Unit tests
   277→uv run pytest tests/flume/ -v
   278→
   279→# Train and evaluate
   280→uv run python scripts/train_flume_v2.py --config default --evaluate
   281→

   283→uv run pytest tests/flume/test_evaluate_vae.py -v
   284→

   286→uv run pytest tests/ -q
   287→```
   288→
   289→### Go/No-Go Gate (Phase 1 → Phase 2)
   290→
   291→Phase 2 proceeds only if ALL Phase 1 green-light criteria are met:
   292→- [ ] KL healthy (128+ active units)
   293→- [ ] Reconstruction fidelity (cos_sim > 0.95)
   294→- [ ] Paraphrase discrimination (P@1 > 0.85)
   295→- [ ] Similarity preservation (ρ > 0.90)
   296→- [ ] Cache A/B positive (FLUME beats hash)
   297→- [ ] Real execution data collection pipeline operational
   298→

   110→**Rule: No destructive operations without learning.** Before removing any code, extract patterns and anti-patterns into the MCP vault. This preserves institutional knowledge for future sessions.
