   113→
   114→| Anti-Pattern | Source File | Lesson |
   115→|-------------|------------|--------|
   116→| Dual VAE architecture | `autoencoder.py` + `training.py` | Two models operating on different input spaces creates integration debt that compounds silently. One coherent model > two partial ones. |
   117→| Training on random noise | `dataset.py` (SyntheticFlumeDataset) | VAE trained on N(0.5, 0.15) learns Gaussian structure, not semantic structure. Synthetic data must have the *kind* of structure you want the model to learn. |
   118→| SHA-256 as semantic embedding | `vae_encoder.py` + `experience_encoder.py` | Cryptographic hashes destroy semantic similarity. "deploy API" and "API deployment" hash to completely unrelated 256D vectors. Never use hash functions where semantic distance matters. |
   119→| Hash-based journey tracking | `journey_tracker.py` | Deterministic text hashing for 12D trajectories means semantically identical tasks map to different points. Observable AI requires *meaningful* representations, not arbitrary ones. |
   120→| Disconnected modules | morphospace, bioelectric, lcsp, etc. | 1,353 lines of code with zero consumers = pure maintenance burden. If a module has no imports, it's not "future work" — it's dead weight. Enforce "no orphan modules" policy. |
   121→| HIHO coherence loss on batch mean | `training.py` line 118-120 | Penalizing `mean(μ) → 0.5` at batch level doesn't ensure per-sample coherence. Regularization must target the right granularity. |
   122→| Character-level tokenizer | `tokenizer.py` + `autoencoder.py` | 74-char vocabulary for semantic embeddings produces poor representations. Always use subword tokenization (or better: pre-trained embeddings). |
   123→| Zombie test processes | System observation | Pytest processes with async tests can hang indefinitely if event loops aren't properly torn down. Always set test timeouts (`pytest-timeout`). |
   124→
   125→**Patterns worth preserving (good ideas in dead code):**
   126→
   127→| Pattern | Source File | Worth Keeping |
   128→|---------|------------|---------------|
   129→| Morphospace stability wells | `morphospace.py` | Concept of "stability wells" around HIHO=0.5 in latent space is sound theory. Re-implement in Phase 2 with real learned latent space. |
   130→| Trajectory prediction with physics | `predictor.py` | Momentum-based prediction and counterfactual branching (`imagine_branches()`) are good Phase 2 features. Preserve the API design. |
   131→| Multi-tier data collection | `experience_collector.py` | Parquet → SurrealDB → Vault → synthetic fallback hierarchy is good architecture. Reuse in new data pipeline. |
   132→| ExperienceEncoder layout | `experience_encoder.py` | The `[12D trajectory | 12 metrics | 5 op_type | ...]` prefix structure is well-designed. Keep for Phase 2, replace only the 227D fingerprint segment. |
   133→| Checkpoint format with config | `training.py` | Saving `{model, optimizer, config, epoch, metrics}` together is correct practice. Carry forward to new training pipeline. |
   134→| LCSP concept | `lcsp.py` | Latent Coherence Stability Predictor concept is valid for Phase 2 — predicting whether a latent point is in a stable region. |
   135→| Bioelectric field modeling | `bioelectric.py` | Mapping latent gradients to "field strength" for action generation is creative. Document for Phase 2 investigation. |
   136→

   139→2. Extract each preservable pattern as a `vault_extract_pattern` (with code examples from the dead modules)

   148→- [ ] Task 0e: Remove 12 dead FLUME modules (1,353 lines)

   157→
   158→## Red Flag → Green Light Summary
   159→
   160→| # | Red Flag | Root Cause | Green Light Criteria | Phase |
   161→|---|----------|-----------|---------------------|-------|
   162→| 1 | KL collapse risk | No KL annealing, trains on noise | KL > 0.1, 128+ active latent units (of 256) | 1 |
   163→| 2 | Reconstruction fidelity unknown | Two VAEs, neither validated | cos_sim(input, reconstructed) > 0.95 on validation set | 1 |
   164→| 3 | Synthetic quality unknown | No generation/evaluation pipeline | Paraphrase Precision@1 > 0.85, Spearman ρ > 0.90 | 1 |
   165→| 4 | Evaluation metrics unvalidated | JourneyTracker uses SHA-256 hashes | Metrics correlate with task performance R² > 0.5 | 1+2 |
   166→| 5 | Integration impact unknown | FLUME only used for cache similarity | A/B: FLUME cache outperforms hash on precision-recall | 1+2 |
   167→| 6 | Universes team fit unclear | No mapping to their research needs | Clear alignment document with 3+ intersection points | 1 |
   168→
   169→---
   170→
   171→## Architecture: Fresh FLUME VAE
   172→
   173→### Core Insight
   174→
   175→Don't learn semantic embeddings from scratch with a tiny model. Use a **pre-trained embedding model** (Ollama `nomic-embed-text`, 768D) as input, then train a **proper VAE** to learn a structured, compressed 256D latent space on top of those embeddings.
   176→
   177→### Phase 1 Architecture: Semantic Cache Engine
   178→
   179→```
   180→Text Input
   181→    │
   182→    ▼
   183→[Ollama nomic-embed-text]  ─── 768D dense semantic embedding
   184→    │
   185→    ▼
   186→[FLUME VAE Encoder]         ─── MLP: 768→512→512→384 → mu(256), logvar(256)
   187→    │
   188→    ▼
   189→256D latent z (mu used as embedding at inference)
   190→    │
   191→    ▼
   192→[FLUME VAE Decoder]         ─── MLP: 256→384→512→512→768 (reconstruct input)
   193→    │
   194→    ▼
   195→[SemanticCache]             ─── cosine similarity matching (threshold ~0.88)
   196→```
   197→
   198→**Why this works:**
   199→- Pre-trained embeddings already capture "deploy API" ≈ "API deployment"
   200→- VAE learns *structured* 256D space where distances are meaningful
   201→- KL regularization ensures smooth latent space (no holes between clusters)
   202→- Decoder forces VAE to retain information (reconstruction validates quality)
   203→- ~2.1M parameters — trains on CPU in minutes
   204→
   205→### Loss Function
   206→
   207→```
   208→L = L_recon + β·L_KL + λ_align·L_contrastive + λ_coh·L_coherence
   209→
   210→L_recon       = MSE(decoded, original_embedding)
   211→L_KL          = -0.5·Σ(1 + logvar - μ² - exp(logvar))    with free-bits per dimension
   212→L_contrastive = InfoNCE over known paraphrase pairs        (τ=0.07)
   213→L_coherence   = (mean(μ) - 0.5)²                          HIHO target
   214→```
   215→
   216→**KL collapse prevention (Red Flag 1):**
   217→- β annealing: 0.0 → 0.1 over first 30% of training steps
   218→- Free-bits: min 0.125 bits/dimension — forces each dimension to encode information
   219→- Active unit monitoring: alert if <128 of 256 dimensions are active
   220→
   221→### Phase 2 Architecture: Reasoning Latent Space
   222→
   223→Extends Phase 1 with a **temporal encoder** over trajectory sequences:
   224→
   225→```
   226→Trajectory Steps [(12D_state, metrics, op_type), ...]
   227→    │
   228→    ▼
   229→[Step Embedding]       ─── Linear(29→128) + positional encoding
   230→    │
   231→    ▼
   232→[Transformer Encoder]  ─── 2 layers, 4 heads, d_model=128
   233→    │
   234→    ▼
   235→[Attention Pooling → mu(256), logvar(256)]
   236→    │
   237→    ▼
   238→[Transformer Decoder]  ─── autoregressive trajectory reconstruction
   239→```
   240→
   241→Enables: interpolation between strategies, novel trajectory sampling, latent space navigation for agent guidance. JourneyTracker transitions from SHA-256 hashing to learned FLUME projections.
   242→
   243→---
   244→
   245→## Training Data Strategy (Hybrid)
   246→
   247→### Bootstrap (Phase 1 — immediate)
   248→
   249→1. **Structured synthetic tasks** (~5,000 samples)
   250→   - Task descriptions across 5 operation types (generate, analyze, search, transform, persist)
   251→   - Template groups with known paraphrases: `["deploy API", "API deployment", "launch API service"]`
   252→   - Each embedded via `nomic-embed-text` → 768D vectors
   253→
   254→2. **Codebase docstrings** (~2,000 samples)
   255→   - Extract from Cohezion source via AST parsing
   256→   - Real domain-specific language
   257→
   258→3. **Vault experiment descriptions** (variable, ~500+ samples)
   259→   - From `~/vaults/cohezion-vault/experiments/*.json`
   260→
   261→4. **Paraphrase generation** (~15,000 pairs)
   262→   - Use Ollama to generate 3 paraphrases per task template
   263→   - Provides contrastive training pairs
   264→
   265→### Fine-tune (accumulates over time)
   266→
   267→5. **Real execution data pipeline**
   268→   - After each compound execution: store `(task_description, operation_type, metrics, skill_name)` as JSONL
   269→   - Path: `data/flume/experiences/execution_log.jsonl`
   270→   - Periodic re-embed + re-train (weekly or on-demand)
   271→
   272→### External (Phase 2)
   273→
   274→6. **Published reasoning datasets** (GSM8K chains, MATH solutions, code generation traces)
   275→   - For trajectory-level training in Phase 2
   276→
   277→---
   278→
   279→## Universes Team Alignment (Red Flag 6)
   280→
   281→**Source:** [Research Engineer, Universes](https://job-boards.greenhouse.io/anthropic/jobs/5061517008)
   282→
   283→### Mapping: Team Needs → FLUME Capabilities
   284→
   285→| Universes Team Need | FLUME Capability | Alignment |
   286→|---------------------|------------------|-----------|
   287→| **Agentic training environments** for extended-duration tasks | Compound engineering loop: 11-step execution pipeline with skill refinement, retrospection, and journey tracking. Literally an RL training environment for agentic tasks. | **Strong** |
   288→| **Evaluation frameworks measuring genuine capability gains** | Red Flag 4 resolution produces a validated evaluation framework with correlation-tested metrics. Phase 1 evaluation suite (reconstruction fidelity, similarity preservation, KL health, cache ablation) demonstrates evaluation engineering rigor. | **Strong** |
   289→| **Navigate ambiguity, manage interruptions, sustain context** | Phase 2 reasoning latent space compresses reasoning trajectories for context sustainability. 256D embeddings preserve semantic meaning across long interactions. Morphospace navigation handles ambiguity via stability wells. | **Strong** (Phase 2) |
   290→| **RL environments and simulation platforms** | 12D universe simulation with FLUME VAE as the representation layer. JourneyTracker records state transitions. DegradationDetector monitors drift. This is RL infrastructure. | **Strong** |
   291→| **Integrate into production training systems** | Phase 1 semantic cache engine is a production system. Demonstrates ability to ship ML infrastructure, not just research prototypes. | **Strong** |
   292→| **Rapidly troubleshoot across research and production ML** | This spec itself — diagnosing architectural fractures, designing fixes, implementing with TDD — demonstrates this capability directly. | **Meta** |
   293→
   294→### Preferred Qualifications Match
   295→
   296→| Qualification | Evidence |
   297→|---------------|----------|
   298→| LLM training/fine-tuning/evaluation | VAE training pipeline, evaluation framework, metric validation |
   299→| RL environments, simulation platforms | 12D universe simulation, compound loop as RL env |
   300→| Large-scale ML infrastructure | SemanticCache, ExperienceCollector, multi-tier data pipeline |
   301→| Containerization, distributed systems | Cloud Run deployment, Tailscale Funnel for MCP, multi-session worktree pattern |
   302→
   303→### Positioning Document (Deliverable)
   304→

   306→1. Frames FLUME as an **agentic training environment** with validated evaluation
   307→2. Highlights the **evaluation engineering** (not just the model)
   308→3. Shows **production readiness** (Phase 1 cache engine in production)
   309→4. Positions the **compound engineering loop** as an RL training methodology
   310→5. Demonstrates research taste: honest red-flag identification, systematic resolution

   380→### Key Integration Points
   381→
   382→**`vae_encoder.py`** (primary integration surface):
   383→```
   384→Current: text → SHA-256 hash → expand to 256D → MLP encoder → mu → 256D
   385→New:     text → Ollama embed → 768D → VAE encoder → mu → 256D
   386→         (fallback: text → SHA-256 hash → expand to 256D if Ollama unavailable)
   387→```
   388→
   389→**`semantic_cache.py`** (consumer — no interface changes):
   390→```
   391→FlumeVAEEmbeddingModel.encode(text) → EmbeddingResult(embedding=[...256 floats...])
   392→# Same interface, better embeddings
   393→```
   394→
   395→**`journey_tracker.py`** (Phase 1 minimal change):
   396→```
   397→text_to_latent(text):
   398→    if flume_encoder available:
   399→        flume_256d = flume_encoder.encode(text)
   400→        return np.tile(flume_256d, 8)  # 256×8 = 2048D for existing pipeline
   401→    else:
   402→        return hash_based_latent(text)  # existing behavior
   403→```

   407→## Verification Strategy

   412→|----------|----------------------------|---------------|
   413→| 1. KL collapse | `uv run python -m scripts.train_flume_v2 --evaluate-kl` | Active units ≥ 128/256, KL > 0.1 |
   414→| 2. Reconstruction | `uv run pytest tests/flume/test_evaluate_vae.py -k reconstruction` | cos_sim(input, recon) > 0.95 on validation |
   415→| 3. Synthetic quality | `uv run pytest tests/flume/test_evaluate_vae.py -k paraphrase` | Precision@1 > 0.85 |
   416→| 4. Metrics validation | `uv run pytest tests/flume/test_evaluate_vae.py -k similarity_preservation` | Spearman ρ > 0.90 between original and latent similarities |
   417→| 5. Integration impact | `uv run pytest tests/flume/test_evaluate_vae.py -k cache_ablation` | FLUME precision-recall dominates hash baseline |
   418→| 6. Team fit | Read positioning document, verify 3+ alignment points | Qualitative: convincing narrative |
   419→
   420→### Full Suite
   421→
   422→```bash
   423→# Unit tests
   424→uv run pytest tests/flume/ -v
   425→
   426→# Train and evaluate
   427→uv run python scripts/train_flume_v2.py --config default --evaluate

   431→

   433→uv run pytest tests/ -q
   434→```
   435→
   436→### Go/No-Go Gate (Phase 1 → Phase 2)
   437→
   438→Phase 2 proceeds only if ALL Phase 1 green-light criteria are met:
   439→- [ ] KL healthy (128+ active units)
   440→- [ ] Reconstruction fidelity (cos_sim > 0.95)
   441→- [ ] Paraphrase discrimination (P@1 > 0.85)
   442→- [ ] Similarity preservation (ρ > 0.90)
   443→- [ ] Cache A/B positive (FLUME beats hash)
   444→- [ ] Real execution data collection pipeline operational

  "plan": "# FLUME VAE: Red Flags → Green Lights\n\n## Core Principles\n\n### 1. Learn Before You Burn\n\n**No destructive operations without extracting knowledge first.** Before removing, replacing, or deprecating any code, data, or artifact:\n\n1. **Identify patterns** — What good ideas exist in the code being removed? What design decisions were sound even if the implementation failed?\n2. **Identify anti-patterns** — What went wrong? What assumptions were incorrect? What traps should future work avoid?\n3. **Store in vault** — Log all patterns via `vault_extract_pattern` and anti-patterns via `vault_log_decision` to the MCP vault. Knowledge compounds; deleted code doesn't.\n4. **Only then proceed** with removal.\n\nThis applies to every phase of this spec, not just Sprint 0. When Phase 2 replaces Phase 1 components, the same discipline applies.\n\n### 2. Evidence Over Assumptions\n\nEvery red flag became a red flag because someone assumed something worked without checking. Every claim in this spec has a concrete verification command. If you can't run a command to prove it, you can't claim it.\n\n### 3. Phased with Hard Gates\n\nNo Phase 2 work begins until ALL Phase 1 green-light criteria are met with evidence. \"Probably fine\" is not a gate-pass.\n\n---\n\n## Context\n\nThe FLUME investigation (2026-02-23) identified 6 red flags — critical unknowns that determine whether FLUME is a viable research artifact or an architectural dead end. Codebase exploration revealed the situation is worse than the investigation assumed: **the system the investigation analyzed doesn't actually exist.** Two incompatible VAE implementations, a broken data pipeline training on random noise, and zero integration with agent behavior mean the red flags aren't \"unknowns to measure\" — they're **architectural problems to solve.**\n\nThis plan starts by freeing system resources (7 GB RAM from zombie processes, 7.4 GB disk from stale artifacts, 2,000+ lines of dead code), then rebuilds FLUME from first principles in two phases, turning each red flag green with concrete, verifiable criteria. It also maps FLUME's capabilities to the Anthropic Universes team Research Engineer role to validate strategic alignment.\n\n---\n\n## Sprint 0: Free System Resources\n\nBefore building anything new, reclaim physical resources and remove dead code. No imports reference them from active code. This preserves institutional knowledge for future sessions.\n\n**Anti-patterns to document (via `vault_log_decision` / `vault_extract_pattern`):**\n\n| Anti-Pattern | Source File | Lesson |\n|-------------|------------|--------|\n| Dual VAE architecture | `autoencoder.py` + `training.py` | Two models operating on different input spaces creates integration debt that compounds silently. One coherent model > two partial ones. |\n| Training on random noise | `dataset.py` (SyntheticFlumeDataset) | VAE trained on N(0.5, 0.15) learns Gaussian structure, not semantic structure. Synthetic data must have the *kind* of structure you want the model to learn. |\n| SHA-256 as semantic embedding | `vae_encoder.py` + `experience_encoder.py` | Cryptographic hashes destroy semantic similarity. \"deploy API\" and \"API deployment\" hash to completely unrelated 256D vectors. Never use hash functions where semantic distance matters. |\n| Hash-based journey tracking | `journey_tracker.py` | Deterministic text hashing for 12D trajectories means semantically identical tasks map to different points. Observable AI requires *meaningful* representations, not arbitrary ones. |\n| Disconnected modules | morphospace, bioelectric, lcsp, etc. | 1,353 lines of code with zero consumers = pure maintenance burden. If a module has no imports, it's not \"future work\" — it's dead weight. Enforce \"no orphan modules\" policy. |\n| HIHO coherence loss on batch mean | `training.py` line 118-120 | Penalizing `mean(μ) → 0.5` at batch level doesn't ensure per-sample coherence. Regularization must target the right granularity. |\n| Character-level tokenizer | `tokenizer.py` + `autoencoder.py` | 74-char vocabulary for semantic embeddings produces poor representations. Always use subword tokenization (or better: pre-trained embeddings). |\n| Zombie test processes | System observation | Pytest processes with async tests can hang indefinitely if event loops aren't properly torn down. Always set test timeouts (`pytest-timeout`). |\n\n**Patterns worth preserving (good ideas in dead code):**\n\n| Pattern | Source File | Worth Keeping |\n|---------|------------|---------------|\n| Morphospace stability wells | `morphospace.py` | Concept of \"stability wells\" around HIHO=0.5 in latent space is sound theory. Re-implement in Phase 2 with real learned latent space. |\n| Trajectory prediction with physics | `predictor.py` | Momentum-based prediction and counterfactual branching (`imagine_branches()`) are good Phase 2 features. Preserve the API design. |\n| Multi-tier data collection | `experience_collector.py` | Parquet → SurrealDB → Vault → synthetic fallback hierarchy is good architecture. Reuse in new data pipeline. |\n| ExperienceEncoder layout | `experience_encoder.py` | The `[12D trajectory | 12 metrics | 5 op_type | ...]` prefix structure is well-designed. Keep for Phase 2, replace only the 227D fingerprint segment. |\n| Checkpoint format with config | `training.py` | Saving `{model, optimizer, config, epoch, metrics}` together is correct practice. Carry forward to new training pipeline. |\n| LCSP concept | `lcsp.py` | Latent Coherence Stability Predictor concept is valid for Phase 2 — predicting whether a latent point is in a stable region. |\n| Bioelectric field modeling | `bioelectric.py` | Mapping latent gradients to \"field strength\" for action generation is creative. Document for Phase 2 investigation. |\n\n**Vault storage plan:**\n1. Log each anti-pattern as a `vault_log_decision` (decision: \"don't do X because Y\")\n2. Extract each preservable pattern as a `vault_extract_pattern` (with code examples from the dead modules)\n3. Log the overall cleanup as a `vault_log_experiment` (hypothesis: \"dead code removal improves system health\")\n\n### Sprint 0 Task List\n\n- [ ] Task 0a: Kill zombie pytest processes (recover 7 GB RAM)\n- [ ] Task 0b: **Extract anti-patterns to vault** (8 anti-patterns from dead code analysis)\n- [ ] Task 0c: **Extract preservable patterns to vault** (7 patterns worth keeping for Phase 2)\n- [ ] Task 0d: Remove stale `venv/`, `data/overnight/`, `data/surrealdb-old/`, `dist/`, `__pycache__` dirs\n- [ ] Task 0e: Remove 12 dead FLUME modules (1,353 lines)\n- [ ] Task 0f: Archive and remove 5 broken FLUME implementations (1,405 lines) to `archive/flume-v1/`\n- [ ] Task 0g: Clean up stale FLUME checkpoints and mass_sim logs\n- [ ] Task 0h: Update `__init__.py` exports after removals\n- [ ] Task 0i: Remove/update tests for removed modules\n- [ ] Task 0j: Run full test suite — confirm zero regressions\n- [ ] Task 0k: **Log cleanup experiment to vault** (what was removed, why, outcomes)\n\n---\n\n## Red Flag → Green Light Summary\n\n| # | Red Flag | Root Cause | Green Light Criteria | Phase |\n|---|----------|-----------|---------------------|-------|\n| 1 | KL collapse risk | No KL annealing, trains on noise | KL > 0.1, 128+ active latent units (of 256) | 1 |\n| 2 | Reconstruction fidelity unknown | Two VAEs, neither validated | cos_sim(input, reconstructed) > 0.95 on validation set | 1 |\n| 3 | Synthetic quality unknown | No generation/evaluation pipeline | Paraphrase Precision@1 > 0.85, Spearman ρ > 0.90 | 1 |\n| 4 | Evaluation metrics unvalidated | JourneyTracker uses SHA-256 hashes | Metrics correlate with task performance R² > 0.5 | 1+2 |\n| 5 | Integration impact unknown | FLUME only used for cache similarity | A/B: FLUME cache outperforms hash on precision-recall | 1+2 |\n| 6 | Universes team fit unclear | No mapping to their research needs | Clear alignment document with 3+ intersection points | 1 |\n\n---\n\n## Architecture: Fresh FLUME VAE\n\n### Core Insight\n\nDon't learn semantic embeddings from scratch with a tiny model. Use a **pre-trained embedding model** (Ollama `nomic-embed-text`, 768D) as input, then train a **proper VAE** to learn a structured, compressed 256D latent space on top of those embeddings.\n\n### Phase 1 Architecture: Semantic Cache Engine\n\n```\nText Input\n    │\n    ▼\n[Ollama nomic-embed-text]  ─── 768D dense semantic embedding\n    │\n    ▼\n[FLUME VAE Encoder]         ─── MLP: 768→512→512→384 → mu(256), logvar(256)\n    │\n    ▼\n256D latent z (mu used as embedding at inference)\n    │\n    ▼\n[FLUME VAE Decoder]         ─── MLP: 256→384→512→512→768 (reconstruct input)\n    │\n    ▼\n[SemanticCache]             ─── cosine similarity matching (threshold ~0.88)\n```\n\n**Why this works:**\n- Pre-trained embeddings already capture \"deploy API\" ≈ \"API deployment\"\n- VAE learns *structured* 256D space where distances are meaningful\n- KL regularization ensures smooth latent space (no holes between clusters)\n- Decoder forces VAE to retain information (reconstruction validates quality)\n- ~2.1M parameters — trains on CPU in minutes\n\n### Loss Function\n\n```\nL = L_recon + β·L_KL + λ_align·L_contrastive + λ_coh·L_coherence\n\nL_recon       = MSE(decoded, original_embedding)\nL_KL          = -0.5·Σ(1 + logvar - μ² - exp(logvar))    with free-bits per dimension\nL_contrastive = InfoNCE over known paraphrase pairs        (τ=0.07)\nL_coherence   = (mean(μ) - 0.5)²                          HIHO target\n```\n\n**KL collapse prevention (Red Flag 1):**\n- β annealing: 0.0 → 0.1 over first 30% of training steps\n- Free-bits: min 0.125 bits/dimension — forces each dimension to encode information\n- Active unit monitoring: alert if <128 of 256 dimensions are active\n\n### Phase 2 Architecture: Reasoning Latent Space\n\nExtends Phase 1 with a **temporal encoder** over trajectory sequences:\n\n```\nTrajectory Steps [(12D_state, metrics, op_type), ...]\n    │\n    ▼\n[Step Embedding]       ─── Linear(29→128) + positional encoding\n    │\n    ▼\n[Transformer Encoder]  ─── 2 layers, 4 heads, d_model=128\n    │\n    ▼\n[Attention Pooling → mu(256), logvar(256)]\n    │\n    ▼\n[Transformer Decoder]  ─── autoregressive trajectory reconstruction\n```\n\nEnables: interpolation between strategies, novel trajectory sampling, latent space navigation for agent guidance. JourneyTracker transitions from SHA-256 hashing to learned FLUME projections.\n\n---\n\n## Training Data Strategy (Hybrid)\n\n### Bootstrap (Phase 1 — immediate)\n\n1. **Structured synthetic tasks** (~5,000 samples)\n   - Task descriptions across 5 operation types (generate, analyze, search, transform, persist)\n   - Template groups with known paraphrases: `[\"deploy API\", \"API deployment\", \"launch API service\"]`\n   - Each embedded via `nomic-embed-text` → 768D vectors\n\n2. **Codebase docstrings** (~2,000 samples)\n   - Extract from Cohezion source via AST parsing\n   - Real domain-specific language\n\n3. **Vault experiment descriptions** (variable, ~500+ samples)\n   - From `~/vaults/cohezion-vault/experiments/*.json`\n\n4. **Paraphrase generation** (~15,000 pairs)\n   - Use Ollama to generate 3 paraphrases per task template\n   - Provides contrastive training pairs\n\n### Fine-tune (accumulates over time)\n\n5. **Real execution data pipeline**\n   - After each compound execution: store `(task_description, operation_type, metrics, skill_name)` as JSONL\n   - Path: `data/flume/experiences/execution_log.jsonl`\n   - Periodic re-embed + re-train (weekly or on-demand)\n\n### External (Phase 2)\n\n6. **Published reasoning datasets** (GSM8K chains, MATH solutions, code generation traces)\n   - For trajectory-level training in Phase 2\n\n---\n\n## Universes Team Alignment (Red Flag 6)\n\n**Source:** [Research Engineer, Universes](https://job-boards.greenhouse.io/anthropic/jobs/5061517008)\n\n### Mapping: Team Needs → FLUME Capabilities\n\n| Universes Team Need | FLUME Capability | Alignment |\n|---------------------|------------------|-----------|\n| **Agentic training environments** for extended-duration tasks | Compound engineering loop: 11-step execution pipeline with skill refinement, retrospection, and journey tracking. Literally an RL training environment for agentic tasks. | **Strong** |\n| **Evaluation frameworks measuring genuine capability gains** | Red Flag 4 resolution produces a validated evaluation framework with correlation-tested metrics. Phase 1 evaluation suite (reconstruction fidelity, similarity preservation, KL health, cache ablation) demonstrates evaluation engineering rigor. | **Strong** |\n| **Navigate ambiguity, manage interruptions, sustain context** | Phase 2 reasoning latent space compresses reasoning trajectories for context sustainability. 256D embeddings preserve semantic meaning across long interactions. Morphospace navigation handles ambiguity via stability wells. | **Strong** (Phase 2) |\n| **RL environments and simulation platforms** | 12D universe simulation with FLUME VAE as the representation layer. JourneyTracker records state transitions. DegradationDetector monitors drift. This is RL infrastructure. | **Strong** |\n| **Integrate into production training systems** | Phase 1 semantic cache engine is a production system. Demonstrates ability to ship ML infrastructure, not just research prototypes. | **Strong** |\n| **Rapidly troubleshoot across research and production ML** | This spec itself — diagnosing architectural fractures, designing fixes, implementing with TDD — demonstrates this capability directly. | **Meta** |\n\n### Preferred Qualifications Match\n\n| Qualification | Evidence |\n|---------------|----------|\n| LLM training/fine-tuning/evaluation | VAE training pipeline, evaluation framework, metric validation |\n| RL environments, simulation platforms | 12D universe simulation, compound loop as RL env |\n| Large-scale ML infrastructure | SemanticCache, ExperienceCollector, multi-tier data pipeline |\n| Containerization, distributed systems | Cloud Run deployment, Tailscale Funnel for MCP, multi-session worktree pattern |\n\n### Positioning Document (Deliverable)\n\nProduce a concise document (max 2 pages) that:\n1. Frames FLUME as an **agentic training environment** with validated evaluation\n2. Highlights the **evaluation engineering** (not just the model)\n3. Shows **production readiness** (Phase 1 cache engine in production)\n4. Positions the **compound engineering loop** as an RL training methodology\n5. Demonstrates research taste: honest red-flag identification, systematic resolution\n\n---\n\n## Implementation Plan\n\n### Phase 1 Scope (This Spec)\n\n**New files:**\n\n| File | Purpose | ~Lines |\n|------|---------|--------|\n| `src/cohezion/flume/vae.py` | Core VAE model (encoder, decoder, reparameterization, loss) | 200 |\n| `src/cohezion/flume/embedding_provider.py` | Pre-trained embedding interface (Ollama + hash fallback) | 120 |\n| `src/cohezion/flume/train_vae.py` | Training pipeline (KL annealing, free-bits, contrastive) | 250 |\n| `src/cohezion/flume/evaluate_vae.py` | Evaluation framework (all red flag metrics) | 200 |\n| `src/cohezion/flume/data_pipeline.py` | Data prep (synthetic tasks, embedding, contrastive pairs) | 180 |\n| `scripts/train_flume_v2.py` | CLI training entry point | 50 |\n| `tests/flume/test_vae_v2.py` | VAE model unit tests | 150 |\n| `tests/flume/test_embedding_provider.py` | Embedding provider tests | 100 |\n| `tests/flume/test_train_vae_v2.py` | Training pipeline tests | 150 |\n| `tests/flume/test_evaluate_vae.py` | Evaluation framework tests | 120 |\n| `tests/flume/test_data_pipeline.py` | Data pipeline tests | 100 |\n\n**Modified files:**\n\n| File | Change | Risk |\n|------|--------|------|\n| `src/cohezion/flume/vae_encoder.py` | Load new VAE model, use EmbeddingProvider. Public interface unchanged. | Low |\n| `src/cohezion/flume/__init__.py` | Add new exports | Low |\n| `src/cohezion/compound/journey_tracker.py` | Optional FLUME encoder path in `text_to_latent()` | Low |\n| `src/cohezion/swarm/semantic_cache.py` | Threshold validation after new model (may need tuning) | Low |\n\n**Removed in Sprint 0** (archived to `archive/flume-v1/`, patterns extracted to vault):\n`autoencoder.py`, `training.py`, `dataset.py`, `journey_finetune_pipeline.py`, `local_finetune_pipeline.py`, plus 12 dead modules.\n\n### Task Breakdown\n\n**Sprint 1: Foundation**\n\n- [ ] Task 1: Create `embedding_provider.py` — Ollama nomic-embed-text wrapper with hash fallback chain\n- [ ] Task 2: Create `vae.py` — FlumeVAE model (encoder, decoder, reparameterization, configurable loss)\n- [ ] Task 3: Create `data_pipeline.py` — synthetic task generation, batch embedding, contrastive pair mining\n- [ ] Task 4: Tests for Tasks 1-3\n\n**Sprint 2: Training**\n\n- [ ] Task 5: Create `train_vae.py` — full training loop with KL annealing, free-bits, contrastive loss, checkpointing\n- [ ] Task 6: Create `scripts/train_flume_v2.py` — CLI entry point\n- [ ] Task 7: Generate bootstrap training data (synthetic tasks + codebase docstrings + vault experiments)\n- [ ] Task 8: Train initial model, validate convergence (loss curves, KL health)\n\n**Sprint 3: Evaluation + Integration**\n\n- [ ] Task 9: Create `evaluate_vae.py` — reconstruction fidelity, similarity preservation, paraphrase discrimination, KL health, cache ablation\n- [ ] Task 10: Run evaluation suite, validate all metrics meet green-light criteria\n- [ ] Task 11: Update `vae_encoder.py` to load new VAE (keep public interface identical)\n- [ ] Task 12: Update `journey_tracker.py` with optional FLUME path\n- [ ] Task 13: Run SemanticCache A/B: FLUME VAE embeddings vs. hash embeddings\n\n**Sprint 4: Data Collection + Positioning**\n\n- [ ] Task 14: Instrument `ExperienceCollector` to log task descriptions for future training\n- [ ] Task 15: Write Universes team positioning document (map FLUME to job description)\n- [ ] Task 16: End-to-end integration test: compound execution → data collection → embedding → cache hit\n\n### Key Integration Points\n\n**`vae_encoder.py`** (primary integration surface):\n```\nCurrent: text → SHA-256 hash → expand to 256D → MLP encoder → mu → 256D\nNew:     text → Ollama embed → 768D → VAE encoder → mu → 256D\n         (fallback: text → SHA-256 hash → expand to 256D if Ollama unavailable)\n```\n\n**`semantic_cache.py`** (consumer — no interface changes):\n```\nFlumeVAEEmbeddingModel.encode(text) → EmbeddingResult(embedding=[...256 floats...])\n# Same interface, better embeddings\n```\n\n**`journey_tracker.py`** (Phase 1 minimal change):\n```\ntext_to_latent(text):\n    if flume_encoder available:\n        flume_256d = flume_encoder.encode(text)\n        return np.tile(flume_256d, 8)  # 256×8 = 2048D for existing pipeline\n    else:\n        return hash_based_latent(text)  # existing behavior\n```\n\n---\n\n## Verification Strategy\n\n### Per Red Flag\n\n| Red Flag | Verification Command/Method | Pass Criteria Synthetic quality | `uv run pytest tests/flume/test_evaluate_vae.py -k paraphrase` | Precision@1 > 0.85 |\n| 4. Metrics validation | `uv run pytest tests/flume/test_evaluate_vae.py -k similarity_preservation` | Spearman ρ > 0.90 between original and latent similarities |\n| 5. Integration impact | `uv run pytest tests/flume/test_evaluate_vae.py -k cache_ablation` | FLUME precision-recall dominates hash baseline |\n| 6. Team fit | Read positioning document, verify 3+ alignment points | Qualitative: convincing narrative |\n\n### Full Suite\n\n```bash\n# Unit tests\nuv run pytest tests/flume/ -v\n\n# Train and evaluate\nuv run python scripts/train_flume_v2.py --config default --evaluate\n\n# Integration test\nuv run pytest tests/flume/test_evaluate_vae.py -v\n\n# Existing test suite (no regressions)\nuv run pytest tests/ -q\n```\n\n### Go/No-Go Gate (Phase 1 → Phase 2)\n\nPhase 2 proceeds only if ALL Phase 1 green-light criteria are met:\n- [ ] KL healthy (128+ active units)\n- [ ] Reconstruction fidelity (cos_sim > 0.95)\n- [ ] Paraphrase discrimination (P@1 > 0.85)\n- [ ] Similarity preservation (ρ > 0.90)\n- [ ] Cache A/B positive (FLUME beats hash)\n- [ ] Real execution data collection pipeline operational\n"

     9→
    10→# Lesson: Experience-Guided Execution Works: Past Session Context Materially Improves Current Session Quality
    11→
    12→## Context
    13→
    14→Sessions that start with injected context from prior sessions consistently outperform cold-start sessions on the same codebase. The experience of prior sessions guides execution quality.
    15→
    16→## Core Learning
    17→
    18→**Always inject prior session context before starting new work. 5 minutes of context loading saves hours of re-discovery.**
    19→
    20→### What Experience Loading Means
    21→```
    22→1. Read continuation file from previous session
    23→2. Query Pilot Memory for recent observations
    24→3. Read recent decisions/ notes (last 5-10)
    25→4. Check current project status in projects/ notes
    26→5. Review active experiments/ notes
    27→
    28→This 5-minute sequence replaces 30-60 minutes of re-orientation.
    29→```
    30→
    31→## Recommendations
    32→
    33→### Do
    34→- Load prior session context before every new session
    35→- Write detailed continuation files at session end
    36→- Save key decisions and discoveries to Pilot Memory immediately
    37→
    38→### Don't
    39→- Start new sessions without context loading (cold sessions are expensive)
    40→- Rely on memory of the previous session (unreliable across sessions)
    41→
    42→## Related Concepts
    43→
    44→- [[compound-engineering]] - Experience-guided execution is the engine of compound knowledge growth
    45→- [[agentic-ai]] - Memory and continuity as first-class agent properties
    46→
    47→## Validation
    48→
    49→**Discovered**: Feb 2026 across multiple compound engineering sessions
    50→**Impact**: 30-60 minute re-orientation eliminated through context loading
    51→**Status**: Validated -- referenced 11 times (high-impact lesson)
    52→

     1→---
     2→title: Holographic Projection Fallback: Dimensionality Reduction Requires Singular Matrix Guard
     3→date: 2026-02-23
     4→severity: MEDIUM
     5→category: ml
     6→tags: [ml, dimensionality-reduction, linear-algebra, fallback, vae]
     7→status: validated
     8→---
     9→
    10→# Lesson: Holographic Projection Fallback: Dimensionality Reduction Requires Singular Matrix Guard
    11→
    12→## Context
    13→
    14→Holographic projection fails when the covariance matrix is singular or near-singular. This occurs with small batches or identical inputs during VAE training.
    15→
    16→## Core Learning
    17→
    18→**All holographic/projection operations must include a fallback for singular matrix cases.**
    19→
    20→### Pattern
    21→```python
    22→def holographic_project(vectors, target_dim):
    23→    try:
    24→        cov = np.cov(vectors.T)
    25→        cov += np.eye(cov.shape[0]) * 1e-6  # epsilon regularization
    26→        eigenvalues, eigenvectors = np.linalg.eigh(cov)
    27→        projection = eigenvectors[:, -target_dim:]
    28→        return vectors @ projection
    29→    except np.linalg.LinAlgError:
    30→        logger.warning("Holographic projection failed -- using truncation fallback")
    31→        return vectors[:, :target_dim]
    32→```
    33→
    34→## Recommendations
    35→
    36→### Do
    37→- Always add epsilon regularization to covariance matrices
    38→- Implement a fallback projection (PCA or truncation)
    39→- Check for NaN in projection output before continuing
    40→
    41→### Don't
    42→- Assume projection will succeed for all batch sizes
    43→- Let LinAlgError propagate through the pipeline
    44→
    45→## Related Concepts
    46→
    47→- [[meta-learning]] - Projection stability enables reliable meta-learning across sessions
    48→
    49→## Validation
    50→
    51→**Discovered**: Feb 2026 during first VAE training run
    52→**Status**: Validated
    53→

    30→---
    31→
    32→# Decision: Operational Principle - No Destructive Operations Without Learning
    33→
    34→**Date**: 2026-02-09 (Session 41 established, Session 42 codified)
    35→**Category**: Operational Governance
    36→**Status**: APPROVED & CODIFIED IN MEMORY.md
    37→**Confidence**: HIGH
    38→
    39→## Decision
    40→
    41→Establish mandatory operational principle: **"No destructive operations without learnings and abstractions applied."**
    42→
    43→This principle applies to all:
    44→- File deletions
    45→- Branch resets or force-pushes
    46→- Database cleanup or data migrations
    47→- Repository restructuring
    48→- Configuration removal
    49→- Deprecation of features
    50→
    51→## Problem Statement
    52→
    53→Previous approach: Ad-hoc cleanup and repository management led to:
    54→- Lost context about why files were created
    55→- Inability to recover patterns or architectural decisions
    56→- Repeated mistakes across sessions
    57→- Institutional knowledge loss
    58→- Difficulty debugging issues that required understanding historical context
    59→
    60→## Solution
    61→
    62→Implement 6-step process before ANY destructive operation:
    63→
    64→1. **DOCUMENT**: Capture current state
    65→   - What exists and how it's structured
    66→   - Dependencies and relationships
    67→   - Who uses it and how
    68→   - Historical context if available
    69→
    70→2. **ANALYZE**: Understand the problem
    71→   - Why is the change needed?
    72→   - What problem does it solve?
    73→   - What alternatives were considered?
    74→   - What are the risks?
    75→
    76→3. **EXTRACT LEARNING**: Preserve knowledge
    77→   - Write findings to vault/MEMORY as pattern or decision
    78→   - Document lessons learned
    79→   - Note what worked and what didn't
    80→   - Identify reusable insights
    81→
    82→4. **CREATE ABSTRACTION**: Build for future
    83→   - If pattern is reusable, implement as utility/template/helper
    84→   - Document the abstraction for team access
    85→   - Make it easy for future sessions to avoid repeating work
    86→
    87→5. **PRESERVE CONTEXT**: Backup before cleanup
    88→   - Create snapshots or archives
    89→   - Document migration path
    90→   - Implement rollback procedures
    91→   - Ensure recoverability
    92→
    93→6. **EXECUTE SAFELY**: Perform the operation
    94→   - Only after all above steps complete
    95→   - With full backup in place
    96→   - Verify safety conditions met
    97→   - Document what changed and why
    98→
    99→## Examples
   100→
   101→### Example 1: File Consolidation
   102→**Destructive Action**: Consolidate 49 Phase 5B documentation files
   103→
   104→**Process Applied**:
   105→1. ✅ DOCUMENT: Catalogued all 49 files, their purposes, and relationships
   106→2. ✅ ANALYZE: Root cause was "documentation forest" - users lost in volume
   107→3. ✅ EXTRACT LEARNING: Created master index pattern, documented navigation hierarchy
   108→4. ✅ CREATE ABSTRACTION: Implemented SESSION_40_MASTER_INDEX.md template for future sessions
   109→5. ✅ PRESERVE CONTEXT: Created docs/session-40-sprint/ archive with all original files
   110→6. ✅ EXECUTE: Safely consolidated with full recoverability
   111→
   112→**Result**: Could reuse consolidation pattern in future, prevented knowledge loss
   113→
   114→### Example 2: Git Cleanup
   115→**Destructive Action**: Delete obsolete feature branches
   116→
   117→**Process**:
   118→1. ✅ DOCUMENT: Record branch purpose, when created, what work was done
   119→2. ✅ ANALYZE: Determine if work should be merged, archived, or discarded
   120→3. ✅ EXTRACT LEARNING: Note patterns in branch lifecycle
   121→4. ✅ CREATE ABSTRACTION: Document branch naming conventions and lifecycle procedures
   122→5. ✅ PRESERVE CONTEXT: Create stash record of final commit before deletion
   123→6. ✅ EXECUTE: Delete with full audit trail
   124→
   125→**Result**: Future branches can follow proven lifecycle pattern
   126→
   127→### Example 3: Data Migration
   128→**Destructive Action**: Migrate from JSONL to SurrealDB for session persistence
   129→
   130→**Process**:
   131→1. ✅ DOCUMENT: Schema of current JSONL format
   132→2. ✅ ANALYZE: Why migration needed (performance, consistency, etc.)
   133→3. ✅ EXTRACT LEARNING: Document data model decisions
   134→4. ✅ CREATE ABSTRACTION: Implement migration utilities as reusable components
   135→5. ✅ PRESERVE CONTEXT: Keep JSONL export for recovery
   136→6. ✅ EXECUTE: Migrate with rollback procedures in place
   137→
   138→**Result**: Migration pattern available for future data structure changes
   139→
   140→## Enforcement
   141→
   142→**Code Review Gate**:
   143→- All destructive operations require review
   144→- Reviewer checks: Are all 6 steps documented?
   145→- No approval until process followed
   146→
   147→**CI/Pre-commit Hooks**:
   148→- Warn on file deletions
   149→- Require commit message mentioning learning
   150→- Block force-pushes to main/develop without approval
   151→
   152→**Documentation**:
   153→- All decisions preserved in vault/MEMORY
   154→- Patterns available for future team members
   155→- Learnings contribute to institutional knowledge
   156→
   157→## Exception Criteria
   158→
   159→Operations authorized by explicit prior approval:
   160→- "Delete these 5 files" authorizes only those 5 files in that scope
   161→- Approval does NOT extend to similar files
   162→- Scope must be explicit and bounded
   163→
   164→## Anti-Patterns
   165→
   166→🚫 **NEVER**: "Just delete this code, it looks unused"
   167→- Might have been deliberately preserved for pattern reuse
   168→- Loss of context prevents future learning
   169→- No extraction of architectural decision
   170→
   171→🚫 **NEVER**: Force-push to fix branch issues without analysis
   172→- Loses commit history
   173→- Prevents future developers from learning from mistakes
   174→- No abstraction of underlying problem
   175→
   176→🚫 **NEVER**: Drop database without export
   177→- Loss of data structure and relationships

    48→---
    49→
    50→## Context
    51→
    52→After implementing 7 phases of journey enrichment (35 tests, 14 files, ~2000 lines), all unit tests passed 100%. However, production experience shows unit tests miss integration bugs:
    53→- Metadata crashes when components interact
    54→- State corruption across component boundaries
    55→- Edge cases only visible in full system context
    56→
    57→Single-agent implementation has blind spots. Different review perspectives find different bug classes.
    58→
    59→## Decision
    60→
    61→Spawn 3 specialized review agents (correctness, test quality, architecture) to perform adversarial review before commit.
    62→
    63→### Protocol
    64→
    65→1. **correctness-reviewer**: Focus on logic bugs, crash scenarios, edge cases
    66→2. **test-quality-reviewer**: Focus on test coverage, assertion quality, mock correctness
    67→3. **architecture-reviewer**: Focus on performance, coupling, design patterns
    68→
    69→Each agent:
    70→- Reviews full diff independently
    71→- Reports findings with severity (CRITICAL, HIGH, MEDIUM, LOW)
    72→- Provides specific file:line references
    73→- Suggests fixes
    74→
    75→All CRITICAL and HIGH findings must be fixed before commit.
    76→
    77→## Chosen Option
    78→
    79→**3-agent parallel adversarial review protocol**
    80→
    81→### Implementation
    82→```python
    83→# Spawn 3 independent reviewers
    84→reviewers = [
    85→    Task(subagent_type="general-purpose", name="correctness-reviewer", ...),
    86→    Task(subagent_type="general-purpose", name="test-quality-reviewer", ...),
    87→    Task(subagent_type="general-purpose", name="architecture-reviewer", ...),
    88→]
    89→
    90→# Each reviews independently (no groupthink)
    91→# Aggregate findings by severity
    92→# Fix all CRITICAL + HIGH before commit
    93→```
    94→
    95→## Alternatives Considered
    96→
    97→### Alternative 1: Single comprehensive review agent
    98→**Rejected**: Single perspective misses bugs. Specialization works better.
    99→
   100→### Alternative 2: Sequential review (one agent at a time)
   101→**Rejected**: Slower. Parallel execution is 3x faster.
   102→
   103→### Alternative 3: More agents (5+)
   104→**Rejected**: Diminishing returns. 3 perspectives (correctness, tests, architecture) cover 90%+ of bugs.
   105→
   106→### Alternative 4: Automated static analysis only
   107→**Rejected**: Static analysis catches syntax/style, not semantic bugs or architectural issues.
   108→
   109→## Decision Reasoning
   110→
   111→### Why This Option?
   112→
   113→1. **Diverse perspectives**: Correctness vs tests vs architecture find different bug classes
   114→2. **Specialization**: Each agent focuses on their domain expertise
   115→3. **Parallel execution**: 3 agents run simultaneously (fast feedback)
   116→4. **Proven effectiveness**: Caught 2 CRITICAL bugs that all tests missed
   117→5. **Cost-effective**: ~15 minutes, zero infrastructure cost
   118→
   119→### Alternatives Rejected
   120→
   121→Single-agent review misses bugs due to limited perspective. Sequential review is slow. More agents have diminishing returns. Static analysis doesn't catch semantic bugs.
   122→
   123→### Confidence Level
   124→
   125→**0.98** - Extremely high confidence. Protocol caught 2 production-crash bugs (metadata=None, stale universe state) that passed all unit tests.
   126→
   127→## Expected Outcomes
   128→
   129→1. Catch 2-5 bugs per 1000 LOC reviewed
   130→2. At least 1 CRITICAL or HIGH finding per major feature
   131→3. Review completes in <30 minutes (3 agents parallel)
   132→4. Zero production crashes from reviewed code
   133→
   134→## Metrics & Impact
   135→
   136→### Estimated
   137→- Review time: 30 minutes (3 agents parallel)
   138→- Bugs found: 3-5 per 1000 LOC
   139→- False positives: <20%
   140→
   141→### Actual (Post-Implementation)
   142→- Review time: 15 minutes (faster than estimated)
   143→- Bugs found: 13 total (2 CRITICAL, 5 HIGH, 6 MEDIUM)
   144→- Distribution:
   145→  - **correctness-reviewer**: 2 CRITICAL (metadata=None crash, stale data), 2 HIGH
   146→  - **test-quality-reviewer**: 3 HIGH (weak assertions, mock issues), 4 MEDIUM
   147→  - **architecture-reviewer**: 1 HIGH (efficiency), 2 MEDIUM
   148→- False positives: 0 (all findings were valid)
   149→- Production crashes prevented: 2
   150→
   151→### Bugs Caught (Session 58)
   152→
   153→**CRITICAL**:
   154→1. `compute_trajectory_quality` crashes with `TypeError: 'NoneType' object is not subscriptable` when `metadata=None`
   155→2. Universe bridge doesn't remove journeys from active list on completion (silent data corruption)
   156→

   164→**MEDIUM**: 6 efficiency and style improvements

Now I have everything for the adversarial review. Let me think through this from three challenger perspectives and update the plan.

**Perspective 1 — Feasibility Challenger:**
- `nomic-embed-text` isn't in the Ollama model list. Need to pull it first.
- 768D→256D is only 3x compression — the bottleneck may not be severe enough to force the VAE to learn meaningful structure.
- ~22,500 training samples for a 2.1M parameter model is overfitting territory.
- Ollama dependency at inference = network call per cache lookup.

**Perspective 2 — Strategic Reviewer:**
- Phase 1 (cache engine) doesn't demonstrate RL capability. Only Phase 2 does. The positioning doc should be written EARLY to guide decisions, not at the end.
- The 5.5M overnight trajectories are irreplaceable Phase 2 training data — **cannot be deleted**.
- The overnight report shows coherence converging to 0.613, improvement staying ~1.0 — real FLUME behavior data.

**Perspective 3 — Risk & Completeness Reviewer:**
- `experience_collector.py`, `experience_encoder.py`, `experience_pipeline.py` are NOT dead code but aren't explicitly marked "keep".
- The vault's 6-step process (ADR from 2026-02-09) requires PRESERVE CONTEXT and CREATE ABSTRACTION steps our plan partially skips.
- Running SurrealDB might have queryable journey data we should export before touching `surrealdb-old/`.

Let me update the plan with all these corrections.

This preserves institutional knowledge for future sessions.\n\n**Anti-patterns to document (via `vault_log_decision` / `vault_extract_pattern`):**\n\n| Anti-Pattern | Source File | Lesson |\n|-------------|------------|--------|\n| Dual VAE architecture | `autoencoder.py` + `training.py` | Two models operating on different input spaces creates integration debt that compounds silently. Synthetic data must have the *kind* of structure you want the model to learn. |\n| SHA-256 as semantic embedding | `vae_encoder.py` + `experience_encoder.py` | Cryptographic hashes destroy semantic similarity. \"deploy API\" and \"API deployment\" hash to completely unrelated 256D vectors. Never use hash functions where semantic distance matters. |\n| Hash-based journey tracking | `journey_tracker.py` | Deterministic text hashing for 12D trajectories means semantically identical tasks map to different points. Observable AI requires *meaningful* representations, not arbitrary ones. |\n| Disconnected modules | morphospace, bioelectric, lcsp, etc. | 1,353 lines of code with zero consumers = pure maintenance burden. If a module has no imports, it's not \"future work\" — it's dead weight. Regularization must target the right granularity. |\n| Character-level tokenizer | `tokenizer.py` + `autoencoder.py` | 74-char vocabulary for semantic embeddings produces poor representations. `__init__.py` | 28 | Module exports — updated after removals |\n\n### Sprint 0 Task List\n\nFollowing the vault's 6-step process (ADR 2026-02-09): DOCUMENT → ANALYZE → EXTRACT → ABSTRACT → PRESERVE → EXECUTE.\n\n- [ ] Task 0a: Kill zombie pytest processes (recover 7 GB RAM)\n- [ ] Task 0b: Install `nomic-embed-text` via Ollama\n- [ ] Task 0c: **DOCUMENT + ANALYZE**: Read all 17 files being removed, catalog what each does\n- [ ] Task 0d: **EXTRACT**: Log 8 anti-patterns to vault via `vault_log_decision`\n- [ ] Task 0e: **EXTRACT**: Log 7 preservable patterns to vault via `vault_extract_pattern`\n- [ ] Task 0f: **ABSTRACT**: Create reusable abstractions where applicable (stability wells concept, multi-tier collection pattern)\n- [ ] Task 0g: **PRESERVE**: Archive broken implementations to `archive/flume-v1/`\n- [ ] Task 0h: **PRESERVE**: Compress overnight journeys.jsonl (zstd), log simulation stats to vault\n- [ ] Task 0i: **PRESERVE**: Query SurrealDB for existing journey data, export if valuable\n- [ ] Task 0j: **EXECUTE**: Remove stale `venv/`, `dist/`, `__pycache__` dirs\n- [ ] Task 0k: **EXECUTE**: Remove 12 dead FLUME modules (1,353 lines)\n- [ ] Task 0l: **EXECUTE**: Remove stale FLUME checkpoints and mass_sim logs\n- [ ] Task 0m: Update `__init__.py` exports after removals\n- [ ] Task 0n: Remove/update tests for removed modules\n- [ ] Task 0o: Run full test suite — confirm zero regressions\n- [ ] Task 0p: **Log cleanup experiment to vault** (what was removed, why, outcomes)\n\n---\n\n## Red Flag → Green Light Summary\n\n| # | Red Flag | Root Cause | Green Light Criteria | Phase |\n|---|----------|-----------|---------------------|-------|\n| 1 | KL collapse risk | No KL annealing, trains on noise | KL > 0.1, 128+ active latent units (of 256) | 1 |\n| 2 | Reconstruction fidelity unknown | Two VAEs, neither validated | cos_sim(input, reconstructed) > 0.95 on validation set | 1 |\n| 3 | Synthetic quality unknown | No generation/evaluation pipeline | Paraphrase Precision@1 > 0.85, Spearman ρ > 0.90 | 1 |\n| 4 | Evaluation metrics unvalidated | JourneyTracker uses SHA-256 hashes | Metrics correlate with task performance R² > 0.5 | 1+2 |\n| 5 | Integration impact unknown | FLUME only used for cache similarity | A/B: FLUME cache outperforms hash on precision-recall | 1+2 |\n| 6 | Universes team fit unclear | No mapping to their research needs | Clear alignment document with 3+ intersection points | 1 |\n\n---\n\n## Architecture: Fresh FLUME VAE\n\n### Core Insight\n\nDon't learn semantic embeddings from scratch with a tiny model. Use a **pre-trained embedding model** (Ollama `nomic-embed-text`, 768D) as input, then train a **proper VAE** to learn a structured, compressed 256D latent space on top of those embeddings.\n\n### Phase 1 Architecture: Semantic Cache Engine\n\n```\nText Input\n    │\n    ▼\n[Ollama nomic-embed-text]  ─── 768D dense semantic embedding\n    │\n    ▼\n[FLUME VAE Encoder]         ─── MLP: 768→512→512→384 → mu(256), logvar(256)\n    │\n    ▼\n256D latent z (mu used as embedding at inference)\n    │\n    ▼\n[FLUME VAE Decoder]         ─── MLP: 256→384→512→512→768 (reconstruct input)\n    │\n    ▼\n[SemanticCache]             ─── cosine similarity matching (threshold ~0.88)\n```\n\n**Why this works:**\n- Pre-trained embeddings already capture \"deploy API\" ≈ \"API deployment\"\n- VAE learns *structured* 256D space where distances are meaningful\n- KL regularization ensures smooth latent space (no holes between clusters)\n- Decoder forces VAE to retain information (reconstruction validates quality)\n- ~2.1M parameters — trains on CPU in minutes\n\n### Loss Function\n\n```\nL = L_recon + β·L_KL + λ_align·L_contrastive + λ_coh·L_coherence\n\nL_recon       = MSE(decoded, original_embedding)\nL_KL          = -0.5·Σ(1 + logvar - μ² - exp(logvar))    with free-bits per dimension\nL_contrastive = InfoNCE over known paraphrase pairs        (τ=0.07)\nL_coherence   = (mean(μ) - 0.5)²                          HIHO target\n```\n\n**KL collapse prevention (Red Flag 1):**\n- β annealing: 0.0 → 0.1 over first 30% of training steps\n- Free-bits: min 0.125 bits/dimension — forces each dimension to encode information\n- Active unit monitoring: alert if <128 of 256 dimensions are active\n\n### Phase 2 Architecture: Reasoning Latent Space\n\nExtends Phase 1 with a **temporal encoder** over trajectory sequences:\n\n```\nTrajectory Steps [(12D_state, metrics, op_type), ...]\n    │\n    ▼\n[Step Embedding]       ─── Linear(29→128) + positional encoding\n    │\n    ▼\n[Transformer Encoder]  ─── 2 layers, 4 heads, d_model=128\n    │\n    ▼\n[Attention Pooling → mu(256), logvar(256)]\n    │\n    ▼\n[Transformer Decoder]  ─── autoregressive trajectory reconstruction\n```\n\nEnables: interpolation between strategies, novel trajectory sampling, latent space navigation for agent guidance. JourneyTracker transitions from SHA-256 hashing to learned FLUME projections.\n\n---\n\n## Training Data Strategy (Hybrid)\n\n### Bootstrap (Phase 1 — immediate)\n\n1. **Structured synthetic tasks** (~5,000 samples)\n   - Task descriptions across 5 operation types (generate, analyze, search, transform, persist)\n   - Template groups with known paraphrases: `[\"deploy API\", \"API deployment\", \"launch API service\"]`\n   - Each embedded via `nomic-embed-text` → 768D vectors\n\n2. **Codebase docstrings** (~2,000 samples)\n   - Extract from Cohezion source via AST parsing\n   - Real domain-specific language\n\n3. **Vault experiment descriptions** (variable, ~500+ samples)\n   - From `~/vaults/cohezion-vault/experiments/*.json`\n\n4. **Paraphrase generation** (~15,000 pairs)\n   - Use Ollama to generate 3 paraphrases per task template\n   - Provides contrastive training pairs\n\n### Fine-tune (accumulates over time)\n\n5. **Real execution data pipeline**\n   - After each compound execution: store `(task_description, operation_type, metrics, skill_name)` as JSONL\n   - Path: `data/flume/experiences/execution_log.jsonl`\n   - Periodic re-embed + re-train (weekly or on-demand)\n\n### External (Phase 2)\n\n6. **Published reasoning datasets** (GSM8K chains, MATH solutions, code generation traces)\n   - For trajectory-level training in Phase 2\n\n---\n\n## Universes Team Alignment (Red Flag 6)\n\n**Source:** [Research Engineer, Universes](https://job-boards.greenhouse.io/anthropic/jobs/5061517008)\n\n### Mapping: Team Needs → FLUME Capabilities\n\n| Universes Team Need | FLUME Capability | Alignment |\n|---------------------|------------------|-----------|\n| **Agentic training environments** for extended-duration tasks | Compound engineering loop: 11-step execution pipeline with skill refinement, retrospection, and journey tracking. Literally an RL training environment for agentic tasks. | **Strong** |\n| **Evaluation frameworks measuring genuine capability gains** | Red Flag 4 resolution produces a validated evaluation framework with correlation-tested metrics. Phase 1 evaluation suite (reconstruction fidelity, similarity preservation, KL health, cache ablation) demonstrates evaluation engineering rigor. | **Strong** |\n| **Navigate ambiguity, manage interruptions, sustain context** | Phase 2 reasoning latent space compresses reasoning trajectories for context sustainability. 256D embeddings preserve semantic meaning across long interactions. Morphospace navigation handles ambiguity via stability wells. | **Strong** (Phase 2) |\n| **RL environments and simulation platforms** | 12D universe simulation with FLUME VAE as the representation layer. Log the overall cleanup as a `vault_log_experiment` (hypothesis: \"dead code removal improves system health\")\n\n### Sprint 0 Task List\n\n- [ ] Task 0a: Kill zombie pytest processes (recover 7 GB RAM)\n- [ ] Task 0b: Install `nomic-embed-text` via Ollama\n- [ ] Task 0c: **EXTRACT**: Log 8 anti-patterns to vault via `vault_log_decision` (from existing broken FLUME code analysis)\n- [ ] Task 0d: **EXTRACT**: Log 7 preservable patterns to vault via `vault_extract_pattern` (stability wells, multi-tier collection, etc.)\n- [ ] Task 0e: Log overnight simulation stats to vault as experiment (5.5M trajectories, convergence data)\n\n---\n\n## Red Flag → Green Light Summary\n\n| # | Red Flag | Root Cause | Green Light Criteria | Phase |\n|---|----------|-----------|---------------------|-------|\n| 1 | KL collapse risk | No KL annealing, trains on noise | KL > 0.1, 128+ active latent units (of 256) | 1 |\n| 2 | Reconstruction fidelity unknown | Two VAEs, neither validated | cos_sim(input, reconstructed) > 0.95 on validation set | 1 |\n| 3 | Synthetic quality unknown | No generation/evaluation pipeline | Paraphrase Precision@1 > 0.85, Spearman ρ > 0.90 | 1 |\n| 4 | Evaluation metrics unvalidated | JourneyTracker uses SHA-256 hashes | Metrics correlate with task performance R² > 0.5 | 1+2 |\n| 5 | Integration impact unknown | FLUME only used for cache similarity | A/B: FLUME cache outperforms hash on precision-recall | 1+2 |\n| 6 | Universes team fit unclear | No mapping to their research needs | Clear alignment document with 3+ intersection points | 1 |\n\n---\n\n## Architecture: Fresh FLUME VAE\n\n### Core Insight\n\nDon't learn semantic embeddings from scratch with a tiny model. Use a **pre-trained embedding model** (Ollama `nomic-embed-text`, 768D) as input, then train a **proper VAE** to learn a structured, compressed 256D latent space on top of those embeddings.\n\n### Phase 1 Architecture: Semantic Cache Engine\n\n```\nText Input\n    │\n    ▼\n[Ollama nomic-embed-text]  ─── 768D dense semantic embedding\n    │\n    ▼\n[FLUME VAE Encoder]         ─── MLP: 768→512→512→384 → mu(256), logvar(256)\n    │\n    ▼\n256D latent z (mu used as embedding at inference)\n    │\n    ▼\n[FLUME VAE Decoder]         ─── MLP: 256→384→512→512→768 (reconstruct input)\n    │\n    ▼\n[SemanticCache]             ─── cosine similarity matching (threshold ~0.88)\n```\n\n**Why this works:**\n- Pre-trained embeddings already capture \"deploy API\" ≈ \"API deployment\"\n- VAE learns *structured* 256D space where distances are meaningful\n- KL regularization ensures smooth latent space (no holes between clusters)\n- Decoder forces VAE to retain information (reconstruction validates quality)\n- ~2.1M parameters — trains on CPU in minutes\n\n### Loss Function\n\n```\nL = L_recon + β·L_KL + λ_align·L_contrastive + λ_coh·L_coherence\n\nL_recon       = MSE(decoded, original_embedding)\nL_KL          = -0.5·Σ(1 + logvar - μ² - exp(logvar))    with free-bits per dimension\nL_contrastive = InfoNCE over known paraphrase pairs        (τ=0.07)\nL_coherence   = (mean(μ) - 0.5)²                          HIHO target\n```\n\n**KL collapse prevention (Red Flag 1):**\n- β annealing: 0.0 → 0.1 over first 30% of training steps\n- Free-bits: min 0.125 bits/dimension — forces each dimension to encode information\n- Active unit monitoring: alert if <128 of 256 dimensions are active\n\n### Phase 2 Architecture: Reasoning Latent Space\n\nExtends Phase 1 with a **temporal encoder** over trajectory sequences:\n\n```\nTrajectory Steps [(12D_state, metrics, op_type), ...]\n    │\n    ▼\n[Step Embedding]       ─── Linear(29→128) + positional encoding\n    │\n    ▼\n[Transformer Encoder]  ─── 2 layers, 4 heads, d_model=128\n    │\n    ▼\n[Attention Pooling → mu(256), logvar(256)]\n    │\n    ▼\n[Transformer Decoder]  ─── autoregressive trajectory reconstruction\n```\n\nEnables: interpolation between strategies, novel trajectory sampling, latent space navigation for agent guidance. JourneyTracker transitions from SHA-256 hashing to learned FLUME projections.\n\n---\n\n## Training Data Strategy (Hybrid)\n\n### Bootstrap (Phase 1 — immediate)\n\n1. **Structured synthetic tasks** (~5,000 samples)\n   - Task descriptions across 5 operation types (generate, analyze, search, transform, persist)\n   - Template groups with known paraphrases: `[\"deploy API\", \"API deployment\", \"launch API service\"]`\n   - Each embedded via `nomic-embed-text` → 768D vectors\n\n2. **Codebase docstrings** (~2,000 samples)\n   - Extract from Cohezion source via AST parsing\n   - Real domain-specific language\n\n3. **Vault experiment descriptions** (variable, ~500+ samples)\n   - From `~/vaults/cohezion-vault/experiments/*.json`\n\n4. **Paraphrase generation** (~15,000 pairs)\n   - Use Ollama to generate 3 paraphrases per task template\n   - Provides contrastive training pairs\n\n### Fine-tune (accumulates over time)\n\n5. **Real execution data pipeline**\n   - After each compound execution: store `(task_description, operation_type, metrics, skill_name)` as JSONL\n   - Path: `data/flume/experiences/execution_log.jsonl`\n   - Periodic re-embed + re-train (weekly or on-demand)\n\n### External (Phase 2)\n\n6. **Published reasoning datasets** (GSM8K chains, MATH solutions, code generation traces)\n   - For trajectory-level training in Phase 2\n\n---\n\n## Universes Team Alignment (Red Flag 6)\n\n**Source:** [Research Engineer, Universes](https://job-boards.greenhouse.io/anthropic/jobs/5061517008)\n\n### Mapping: Team Needs → FLUME Capabilities\n\n| Universes Team Need | FLUME Capability | Alignment |\n|---------------------|------------------|-----------|\n| **Agentic training environments** for extended-duration tasks | Compound engineering loop: 11-step execution pipeline with skill refinement, retrospection, and journey tracking. execution → data collection → embedding → cache hit\n\n### Key Integration Points\n\n**`vae_encoder.py`** (primary integration surface):\n```\nCurrent: text → SHA-256 hash → expand to 256D → MLP encoder → mu → 256D\nNew:     text → Ollama embed → 768D → VAE encoder → mu → 256D\n         (fallback: text → SHA-256 hash → expand to 256D if Ollama unavailable)\n```\n\n**`semantic_cache.py`** Phased with Hard Gates\n\nNo Phase 2 work begins until ALL Phase 1 green-light criteria are met with evidence. \"Probably fine\" is not a gate-pass.\n\n---\n\n## Context\n\nThe FLUME investigation (2026-02-23) identified 6 red flags — critical unknowns that determine whether FLUME is a viable research artifact or an architectural dead end. Codebase exploration revealed the situation is worse than the investigation assumed: **the system the investigation analyzed doesn't actually exist.** Two incompatible VAE implementations, a broken data pipeline training on random noise, and zero integration with agent behavior mean the red flags aren't \"unknowns to measure\" — they're **architectural problems to solve.**\n\nThis plan starts by freeing system resources (7 GB RAM from zombie processes, 7.4 GB disk from stale artifacts, 2,000+ lines of dead code), then rebuilds FLUME from first principles in two phases, turning each red flag green with concrete, verifiable criteria. It also maps FLUME's capabilities to the Anthropic Universes team Research Engineer role to validate strategic alignment.\n\n---\n\n## Sprint 0: Free RAM + Extract Knowledge\n\nNo file deletions needed — plenty of disk space. Focus on reclaiming RAM and extracting knowledge from existing code before building new.\n\n### Physical Resources\n\n| Resource | What | Recovery | Command |\n|----------|------|----------|---------|\n| **7 GB RAM** | 12 zombie pytest processes running 25+ hours | Kill all | `kill 286767 286775 288864 288872 290708 290714 290718 293426 293432 293435 294452 294457` |\n\n### Prerequisite: Install nomic-embed-text\n\n```bash\nollama pull nomic-embed-text\n```\n\n### Valuable Data Discovered (preserved, not deleted)\n\n| Data | Size | Value |\n|------|------|-------|\n| `data/overnight/journeys.jsonl` | 3.2 GB, 5.5M records | Real 12D simulation trajectories — Phase 2 training data |\n| `data/overnight/checkpoints/` | 200 JSON files | Simulation convergence history (coherence 0.568 → 0.613) |\n| `data/flume/checkpoints/flume_vae_ep*.pt` | 4 checkpoints | Noise-trained model weights — reference for comparison |\n| `data/surrealdb-old/` | 28 MB | Old SurrealDB instance — may contain queryable journey data |\n\n### Sprint 0 Verification\n\n```bash\nfree -h                                # Confirm RAM recovered\npython -c \"from cohezion.flume import FlumeVAEEncoder\"  # Core import works\n```\n\n### Knowledge Extraction (BEFORE any deletions)\n\n**Rule: No destructive operations without learning.** Before removing any code, extract patterns and anti-patterns into the MCP vault. This preserves institutional knowledge for future sessions.\n\n**Anti-patterns to document (via `vault_log_decision` / `vault_extract_pattern`):**\n\n| Anti-Pattern | Source File | Lesson |\n|-------------|------------|--------|\n| Dual VAE architecture | `autoencoder.py` + `training.py` | Two models operating on different input spaces creates integration debt that compounds silently. One coherent model > two partial ones. |\n| Training on random noise | `dataset.py` (SyntheticFlumeDataset) | VAE trained on N(0.5, 0.15) learns Gaussian structure, not semantic structure. Synthetic data must have the *kind* of structure you want the model to learn. |\n| SHA-256 as semantic embedding | `vae_encoder.py` + `experience_encoder.py` | Cryptographic hashes destroy semantic similarity. \"deploy API\" and \"API deployment\" hash to completely unrelated 256D vectors. Never use hash functions where semantic distance matters. |\n| Hash-based journey tracking | `journey_tracker.py` | Deterministic text hashing for 12D trajectories means semantically identical tasks map to different points. Observable AI requires *meaningful* representations, not arbitrary ones. |\n| Disconnected modules | morphospace, bioelectric, lcsp, etc. | 1,353 lines of code with zero consumers = pure maintenance burden. If a module has no imports, it's not \"future work\" — it's dead weight. Enforce \"no orphan modules\" policy. |\n| HIHO coherence loss on batch mean | `training.py` line 118-120 | Penalizing `mean(μ) → 0.5` at batch level doesn't ensure per-sample coherence. Regularization must target the right granularity. |\n| Character-level tokenizer | `tokenizer.py` + `autoencoder.py` | 74-char vocabulary for semantic embeddings produces poor representations. Always use subword tokenization (or better: pre-trained embeddings). |\n| Zombie test processes | System observation | Pytest processes with async tests can hang indefinitely if event loops aren't properly torn down. Always set test timeouts (`pytest-timeout`). |\n\n**Patterns worth preserving (good ideas in dead code):**\n\n| Pattern | Source File | Worth Keeping |\n|---------|------------|---------------|\n| Morphospace stability wells | `morphospace.py` | Concept of \"stability wells\" around HIHO=0.5 in latent space is sound theory. Re-implement in Phase 2 with real learned latent space. |\n| Trajectory prediction with physics | `predictor.py` | Momentum-based prediction and counterfactual branching (`imagine_branches()`) are good Phase 2 features. Preserve the API design. |\n| Multi-tier data collection | `experience_collector.py` | Parquet → SurrealDB → Vault → synthetic fallback hierarchy is good architecture. Reuse in new data pipeline. |\n| ExperienceEncoder layout | `experience_encoder.py` | The `[12D trajectory | 12 metrics | 5 op_type | ...]` prefix structure is well-designed. Keep for Phase 2, replace only the 227D fingerprint segment. |\n| Checkpoint format with config | `training.py` | Saving `{model, optimizer, config, epoch, metrics}` together is correct practice. Carry forward to new training pipeline. |\n| LCSP concept | `lcsp.py` | Latent Coherence Stability Predictor concept is valid for Phase 2 — predicting whether a latent point is in a stable region. |\n| Bioelectric field modeling | `bioelectric.py` | Mapping latent gradients to \"field strength\" for action generation is creative. Document for Phase 2 investigation. |\n\n**Vault storage plan:**\n1. Log each anti-pattern as a `vault_log_decision` (decision: \"don't do X because Y\")\n2. Extract each preservable pattern as a `vault_extract_pattern` (with code examples from the dead modules)\n3. Log the overall cleanup as a `vault_log_experiment` (hypothesis: \"dead code removal improves system health\")\n\n### Sprint 0 Task List\n\n- [ ] Task 0a: Kill zombie pytest processes (recover 7 GB RAM)\n- [ ] Task 0b: Install `nomic-embed-text` via Ollama\n- [ ] Task 0c: **EXTRACT**: Log 8 anti-patterns to vault via `vault_log_decision` (from existing broken FLUME code analysis)\n- [ ] Task 0d: **EXTRACT**: Log 7 preservable patterns to vault via `vault_extract_pattern` (stability wells, multi-tier collection, etc.)\n- [ ] Task 0e: Log overnight simulation stats to vault as experiment (5.5M trajectories, convergence data)\n\n---\n\n## Red Flag → Green Light Summary\n\n| # | Red Flag | Root Cause | Green Light Criteria | Phase |\n|---|----------|-----------|---------------------|-------|\n| 1 | KL collapse risk | No KL annealing, trains on noise | KL > 0.1, 128+ active latent units (of 256) | 1 |\n| 2 | Reconstruction fidelity unknown | Two VAEs, neither validated | cos_sim(input, reconstructed) > 0.95 on validation set | 1 |\n| 3 | Synthetic quality unknown | No generation/evaluation pipeline | Paraphrase Precision@1 > 0.85, Spearman ρ > 0.90 | 1 |\n| 4 | Evaluation metrics unvalidated | JourneyTracker uses SHA-256 hashes | Metrics correlate with task performance R² > 0.5 | 1+2 |\n| 5 | Integration impact unknown | FLUME only used for cache similarity | A/B: FLUME cache outperforms hash on precision-recall | 1+2 |\n| 6 | Universes team fit unclear | No mapping to their research needs | Clear alignment document with 3+ intersection points | 1 |\n\n---\n\n## Architecture: Fresh FLUME VAE\n\n### Core Insight\n\nDon't learn semantic embeddings from scratch with a tiny model. Use a **pre-trained embedding model** (Ollama `nomic-embed-text`, 768D) as input, then train a **proper VAE** to learn a structured, compressed 256D latent space on top of those embeddings.\n\n### Phase 1 Architecture: Semantic Cache Engine\n\n```\nText Input\n    │\n    ▼\n[Ollama nomic-embed-text]  ─── 768D dense semantic embedding\n    │\n    ▼\n[FLUME VAE Encoder]         ─── MLP: 768→512→512→384 → mu(256), logvar(256)\n    │\n    ▼\n256D latent z (mu used as embedding at inference)\n    │\n    ▼\n[FLUME VAE Decoder]         ─── MLP: 256→384→512→512→768 (reconstruct input)\n    │\n    ▼\n[SemanticCache]             ─── cosine similarity matching (threshold ~0.88)\n```\n\n**Why this works:**\n- Pre-trained embeddings already capture \"deploy API\" ≈ \"API deployment\"\n- VAE learns *structured* 256D space where distances are meaningful\n- KL regularization ensures smooth latent space (no holes between clusters)\n- Decoder forces VAE to retain information (reconstruction validates quality)\n- ~2.1M parameters — trains on CPU in minutes\n\n### Loss Function\n\n```\nL = L_recon + β·L_KL + λ_align·L_contrastive + λ_coh·L_coherence\n\nL_recon       = MSE(decoded, original_embedding)\nL_KL          = -0.5·Σ(1 + logvar - μ² - exp(logvar))    with free-bits per dimension\nL_contrastive = InfoNCE over known paraphrase pairs        (τ=0.07)\nL_coherence   = (mean(μ) - 0.5)²                          HIHO target\n```\n\n**KL collapse prevention (Red Flag 1):**\n- β annealing: 0.0 → 0.1 over first 30% of training steps\n- Free-bits: min 0.125 bits/dimension — forces each dimension to encode information\n- Active unit monitoring: alert if <128 of 256 dimensions are active\n\n### Phase 2 Architecture: Reasoning Latent Space\n\nExtends Phase 1 with a **temporal encoder** over trajectory sequences:\n\n```\nTrajectory Steps [(12D_state, metrics, op_type), ...]\n    │\n    ▼\n[Step Embedding]       ─── Linear(29→128) + positional encoding\n    │\n    ▼\n[Transformer Encoder]  ─── 2 layers, 4 heads, d_model=128\n    │\n    ▼\n[Attention Pooling → mu(256), logvar(256)]\n    │\n    ▼\n[Transformer Decoder]  ─── autoregressive trajectory reconstruction\n```\n\nEnables: interpolation between strategies, novel trajectory sampling, latent space navigation for agent guidance. JourneyTracker transitions from SHA-256 hashing to learned FLUME projections.\n\n---\n\n## Training Data Strategy (Hybrid)\n\n### Bootstrap (Phase 1 — immediate)\n\n1. **Structured synthetic tasks** (~5,000 samples)\n   - Task descriptions across 5 operation types (generate, analyze, search, transform, persist)\n   - Template groups with known paraphrases: `[\"deploy API\", \"API deployment\", \"launch API service\"]`\n   - Each embedded via `nomic-embed-text` → 768D vectors\n\n2. **Codebase docstrings** (~2,000 samples)\n   - Extract from Cohezion source via AST parsing\n   - Real domain-specific language\n\n3. **Vault experiment descriptions** (variable, ~500+ samples)\n   - From `~/vaults/cohezion-vault/experiments/*.json`\n\n4. **Paraphrase generation** (~15,000 pairs)\n   - Use Ollama to generate 3 paraphrases per task template\n   - Provides contrastive training pairs\n\n### Fine-tune (accumulates over time)\n\n5. **Real execution data pipeline**\n   - After each compound execution: store `(task_description, operation_type, metrics, skill_name)` as JSONL\n   - Path: `data/flume/experiences/execution_log.jsonl`\n   - Periodic re-embed + re-train (weekly or on-demand)\n\n### External (Phase 2)\n\n6. **Published reasoning datasets** (GSM8K chains, MATH solutions, code generation traces)\n   - For trajectory-level training in Phase 2\n\n---\n\n## Universes Team Alignment (Red Flag 6)\n\n**Source:** [Research Engineer, Universes](https://job-boards.greenhouse.io/anthropic/jobs/5061517008)\n\n### Mapping: Team Needs → FLUME Capabilities\n\n| Universes Team Need | FLUME Capability | Alignment |\n|---------------------|------------------|-----------|\n| **Agentic training environments** for extended-duration tasks | Compound engineering loop: 11-step execution pipeline with skill refinement, retrospection, and journey tracking.
